Quasi-toposes as elementary quotient completions
Maria Emilia Maietti, Fabio Pasquali, Giuseppe Rosolini

Abstract
The elementary quotient completion of an elementary doctrine in the sense of Lawvere was introduced in previous work by the first and third authors. It generalises the exact completion of a category with finite products and weak equalisers. In this paper we characterise when an elementary quotient completion is a quasi-topos. We obtain as a corollary a complete characterisation of when an elementary quotient completions is an elementary topos. As a byproduct we determine also when the elementary quotient completion of a tripos is equivalent to the doctrine obtained via the tripos-to-topos construction. Our results are reminiscent of other works regarding exact completions and put those under a common scheme: in particular, Carboni and Vitale’s characterisation of exact completions in terms of their projective objects, Carboni and Rosolini’s characterisation of locally cartesian closed exact completions, also in the revision by Emmenegger, and Menni’s characterisation of the exact completions which are elementary toposes.



The paper contains results presented by the authors at several international
meetings in the past years, in particular at Logic Colloquium 2016 and
Category Theory 2017, and during the Trimester devoted to Types, Homotopy
Type Theory and Verification at the Hausdorff research Institute for
Mathematics in 2018. We would like to thank the organisers of these events
who gave us the opportunity to present our results.

1
Introduction

The study of constructions for completing a category with quotients is a
central topic not only in mathematics but also in computer science.
In category theory a well-known related notion is that of exact completion of
a category with finite limits, and that of a regular category, which has been
widely studied and applied; see
[
CC82
,
CV98
]
.

In
[
MR13b
]
the first and third authors generalized the notion
of exact completion on a category with weak finite limits to that of an
elementary quotient completion
of a Lawvere’s elementary
doctrine
[
Law69
,
Law70
]

as an universal construction to close such a doctrine with respect to a
suitable notion of quotient.

The exact completion of a category
\mathpzc{C}
with finite products and weak
limits is an instance of such a construction in the sense that its subobject
doctrine is the elementary quotient completion doctrine of the doctrine of
variations of
\mathpzc{C}

[
Gra00
]
.

In the paper we study elementary quotient completions performed on the
special class of Lawvere’s elementary doctrines called
triposes
,
introduced in
[
HJP80
]
, to build elementary toposes by means of
what is now known as the tripos-to-topos construction; see
[
Fre15
]
.
We then characterize those triposes whose elementary quotient completion has
an
arithmetic quasi-topos
—
i.e.
a quasi-topos equipped with a
natural number object—as a base category.

To obtain the characterization, we extend some known results about exact
completions such as Carboni and Vitale’s characterization of exact
completions in terms of its projective objects in
[
CV98
]
,
Menni’s characterization of the exact completions which are toposes in

[
Men03
]
and Carboni and Rosolini’s characterization of the
locally cartesian closed exact completions
[
CR00
]
. In
particular, we show that

•

an elementary doctrine

P:\mathbb{C}^{op}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
closed under
effective quotients is the elementary quotient completion of the
doctrine determined by the restriction of
P
to the full
subcategory of
\mathbb{C}
on its projective objects;

•

the base category of the elementary quotient
completion of
P
turns weak universal properties of

\mathbb{C}
into (strong) universal properties of the base of the
elementary quotient completion. Those include binary coproducts, a
natural number object, a parametrized list object, a subobject
classifier, a cartesian closed structure, a locally cartesian
structure.

•

by using results in
[
MR16
]
we characterize when an elementary
quotient completion is an elementary topos;

•

by using results in
[
MPR17
]
we characterize when an elementary quotient completion
is a tripos-to-topos construction.

We conclude by pointing out some relevant examples of arithmetic
quasi-toposes arising as non-exact elementary quotient completions. Most
notably they include the category of equilogical spaces of

[
Sco76
,
Sco96
,
BBS04
]
,
that of assemblies over a partial combinatory algebra (see

[
Hyl82
,
vO08
]
), and
the category of total setoids, in the style of E. Bishop, over
Coquand and Paulin’s Calculus of Inductive Constructions which is the
theory at the base of the proof-assistant Coq.

2
Preliminary definitions on doctrines and completions

This section collects the necessary definitions to introduce the

elementary quotient completion
of an elementary doctrine and related
properties of a doctrine. Recall the following notions from

[
MR13a
]
; see also
[
EPR20
]
.

2.1 Definition
.

A
primary doctrine
is an indexed poset

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

such that

(i)

\mathpzc{C}
is a category with a terminal object
T
and binary
products

\textstyle{C_{1}}
\textstyle{C_{1}\times C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}}
\textstyle{C_{2}}

for each pair of objects
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
;

(ii)

each fibre
P(C)
is an inf-semilattice

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{P(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\top_{C}}
\textstyle{\land_{C}}
\textstyle{P(C)\times P(C)}

(iii)

and the operations are natural.

As usual with an indexed category, the category
\mathpzc{C}
is often called

base
of the doctrine. We say that
\alpha
is
over

A
if

\alpha
is an element of
P(A)
.
We write
A_{1}\times A_{2}\times\ldots\times A_{n}
for an arbitrary finite
product with factors
A_{1}
,
A_{2}
,…,
A_{n}
.

2.2 Examples
.

(a)
An example of primary doctrine comes directly from first-order logic is the
Lindenbaum–Tarski
algebras of well-formed formulas of a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order
language
\mathscr{L}
. The base category is the category
\mathpzc{V}
of lists of
distinct variables and term substitutions, and the primary doctrine

LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
on
\mathpzc{V}
is given on a list of
variables
\vec{x}
by taking
LT(\vec{x})
as the Lindenbaum–Tarski
algebra of well-formed formulas with free variables in
\vec{x}
. Meets in

LT(\vec{x})
are given by conjunctions while the top element by any true
formula; see
[
MR13a
]
for more details.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be an inf-semilattice. The functor
\mathcal{P}{H}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
sending a set
A
to
\mathpzc{H}^{A}
and a function
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
to
\mathcal{P}{H}(f)=-\circ f
is a primary doctrine.

(c)
If
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits the functor
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a primary doctrine.

(d)
Another categorical example is given by a category
\mathpzc{C}
with binary products and
weak pullbacks, by defining
the doctrine functor of
variations

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.29405pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

which evaluates as the poset reflection of each comma category

\mathpzc{C}/A
at each object
A
of
\mathpzc{C}
, introduced in

[
Gra00
]
.

2.3 Definition
.

Primary doctrines are the objects of the 2-category
PD
where

the 1-morphisms

in
PD
are pairs
(F,b)
where
F:\mathpzc{C}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{D}

is a functor and
b:P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.98851pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}}R\circ F^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}
is a natural
transformation as in the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{F^{\textrm{\tiny op}}}
POS
\textstyle{{\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R}
\textstyle{b}
\textstyle{\kern-1.72218pt\cdot}

where the functor
F
preserves products

the 2-morphisms

are natural transformations

\theta:F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle.}}{{\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}}G
such that

\textstyle{{\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{F^{\textrm{\tiny op}}}
\textstyle{G^{\textrm{\tiny op}}}
POS
\textstyle{{\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R}
\textstyle{b\kern 2.15277pt\cdot\kern-2.15277pt}
\textstyle{\kern-2.15277pt\cdot\kern 2.15277ptc}
.
\textstyle{\theta^{\textrm{\tiny op}}}
\textstyle{\leq}

so that, for every
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
\alpha
in
P(A)
,
one has

b_{A}(\alpha)\leq_{F(A)}R_{\theta_{A}}(c_{A}(\alpha))
.

2.4 Example
.

A set-theoretic model
\mathfrak{M}
for a first-order theory
\mathscr{T}

determines a 1-arrow from the primary doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
to the
primary doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{Set}}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
in
PD
. And a
homomorphism
f:\mathfrak{M}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
between two set-theoretic
models of
\mathscr{T}
gives rise to a 2-arrow; see

[
MR13b
]
.

Given a doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
, a category
\mathpzc{D}
with finite products and a functor
F:\mathpzc{D}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{C}
that preserves products, the composition of
P
with
F^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}:\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}
gives a doctrine
PF^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}:\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

called
change of base of
P
along
F
.

2.5 Definition
.

An
elementary doctrine on
\mathpzc{C}

is a primary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
such that for every object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
, there is an object
\delta_{A}
over
A\times A
such that for every arrow

e
of the form
<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>:X\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X\times A\times A

in
\mathpzc{C}
, the assignment

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{e}(\alpha)\mathchar 12346\relax=P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\alpha)\wedge_{X\times A\times A}P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\delta_{A})

for
\alpha
in
P(X\times A)
determines a left adjoint to

P_{e}:P(X\times A\times A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(X\times A).

It follows that the assignment

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{<\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{A}>}(\alpha)\mathchar 12346\relax=P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\alpha)\wedge_{A\times A}\delta_{A}

for
\alpha
in
P(A)
determines a left adjoint to

P_{<\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{A}>}:P(A\times A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A)

This means that
\delta_{A}
is determined uniquely for each
object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and hence we will refert to
\delta_{A}
as the

fibred equality on
A
.

2.6 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is said to be
extensional
if
for every pair of parallel arrows
f,g:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
in
\mathpzc{C}
it holds that
f=g
if and only if
\top_{X}=P_{<f,g>}(\delta_{A})
.

The change of base of an elementary doctrine is again elementary, but the new elementary doctrine might fail to be extensional.

2.7 Examples
.

(a)
Consider a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
and the associated primary doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(a). The doctrine
LT
is elementary if and only the equality is definable in
\mathscr{T}
.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be an inf-semilattice. The primary doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(b) is elementary if and only if
\mathpzc{H}
has a bottom element (see
[
EPR20
]
). In this case
\delta_{A}
is the function that maps
(a,a^{\prime})
to
\top
if
a=a^{\prime}
and to
\bot
otherwise. In this case
\mathcal{P}{H}
has comprehensive diagonals.

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits.
The doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(c) is elementary with comprehensive diagonals where
\delta_{A}
is represented by the diagonal on
A
.
(d)
Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits and weak pullbacks. The doctrine

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.88197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(d) is elementary with comprehensive diagonals where
\delta_{A}
is represented by the diagonal on
A
.

2.8 Definition
.

Elementary doctrines are the object of
ED
, the 2-full subcategory
PD
whose 1-morphisms are those 1-morphism
(F,b):P\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}R
of
PD
such that for every object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
, the functor
b_{A}:P(A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.57643pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}R(F(A))
commutes with the left adjoints

\textstyle{P(X\times A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{e}}
\textstyle{b_{X\times A}}
\textstyle{R(F(X\times A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{R\langle F\mathrm{pr}_{1},F\mathrm{pr}_{2}\rangle}
\textstyle{R(FX\times FA)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{e^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{P(X\times A\times A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{X\times A\times A}}
\textstyle{R(F(X\times A\times A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{R\langle F\mathrm{pr}_{1},F\mathrm{pr}_{2},F\mathrm{pr}_{3}\rangle}
\textstyle{R(FX\times FA\times FA)}

where
e
is
<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>:X\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X\times A\times A
and
e^{\prime}
is
<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>:FX\times FA\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}FX\times FA\times FA
.

We recall from
[
MR13a
]
that it is possible to add comprehensive diagonals to an elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}
as follows.

2.9 Definition
.

Consider the category
\mathpzc{X}_{P}
, the “
extensional collapse
” of

P
whose objects are are the objects of
\mathpzc{C}
and where an arrow
\left[{f}\right]:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
is an equivalence class
of morphisms
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
in
\mathpzc{C}
such that

\delta_{A}\leq_{A\times A}P_{f\times f}(\delta_{B})
in
P(A\times A)
with
respect to the equivalence which relates
f
and
f^{\prime}
when

\delta_{A}\leq_{A\times A}P_{f\times f^{\prime}}(\delta_{B})
. Composition is given by that of
\mathpzc{C}
on representatives, and
identities are represented by identities of
\mathpzc{C}
. We then define the doctrine

{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}:\mathpzc{X}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}

on
\mathpzc{X}_{P}
essentially as
P
itself.

2.10 Definition
.

Let
ExD
denote the full subcategory of
ED
on elementary doctrines with comprehensive diagonals.

2.11 Proposition
.

There is left biadjoint to the inclusion of
ExD
into
ED
which on objects associates the doctrine
{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}::\mathpzc{X}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}
to an elementary doctrine

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}
.

2.12 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.89914pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 92.89914pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is

existential
when, for
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
in
\mathpzc{C}
, for a(ny)
projection
\mathrm{pr}_{i}:A_{1}\times A_{2}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A_{i}
,
i=1,2
, the functor

P_{\mathrm{pr}_{i}}:P(A_{i})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.5936pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A_{1}\times A_{2})
has a left adjoint

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{i}}
—we shall call such a left adjoint
existential
—and those left adjoints satisfy the

Beck-Chevalley Condition
:

for any pullback diagram

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}}
\textstyle{A}

with
\mathrm{pr}
a projection (hence also
\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}
a projection), for any

\beta
in
P(X)
, the natural inequality

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}}P_{f^{\prime}}(\beta)\leq P_{f}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}}(\beta)
in
P(A^{\prime})
is
an identity;

Frobenius Reciprocity
:

for
\mathrm{pr}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
a projection,

\alpha
in
P(A)
,
\beta
in
P(X)
, the natural inequality

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}}(P_{\mathrm{pr}}(\alpha)\wedge\beta)\leq\alpha\wedge\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}}(\beta)
in

P(A)
is an identity.

2.13 Examples
.

(a)
Consider a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
and the associated primary doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(a). The doctrine
LT
is existential where left adjoints along projections are given by the existential quantification.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be an inf-semilattice. The primary doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is existential if and only if
\mathpzc{H}
is a frame (see
[
EPR20
]
).

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits.
The doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(c) is existential if and only if
\mathpzc{C}
is regular (see
[
Jac99
]
).

(d)
Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits and weak pullbacks. The doctrine

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(d) is existential where left adjoint are given by composition.

2.14 Remark
.

As shown by Lawvere, in an elementary existential doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
every map of the form
P_{f}
has a left adjoint
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}
. For
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
the maps
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}:P(A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(B)
is

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}(\alpha):=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}[P_{f\times\mathrm{id}_{B}}(\delta_{B})\wedge P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\alpha)]

see also
[
Pit02
]
. Moreover, these left adjoints satisfy the Frobenius Reciprocity in the sense that for every
\beta
in
P(B)
it holds
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}(\alpha)\wedge\beta=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}(\alpha\wedge P_{f}(\beta))
. But they not necessarily satisfy the Beck-Chevalley condition (see
[
MT23
]
for a contraexample).

This allows to give a quick description of the 2-category
EED
, which is the 2-full subcategory of
ED
on those elementary doctrines that are existential with comprehensive diagonals and whose 1-morphisms are those
(F,b):P\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}R
such that for every
f:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
in
\mathpzc{C}
the following commute

\textstyle{P(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}}
\textstyle{R(FX)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{Ff}}
\textstyle{P(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{A}}
\textstyle{R(FA)}

The full subcategory of
EED
on those existential elementary doctrines with comprehensive diagonals is
EExD
.

A primary doctrine

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}

is
implicational
if, for every object
A
in

\mathpzc{C}
, every
\alpha
in
P(A)
, the functor

\alpha\wedge\mathrm{-}:P(A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A)

has a right adjoint
\alpha\Rightarrow\mathrm{-}:P(A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A)
.
A primary doctrine

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}

is
disjunctive

if every
P(A)
has finite distributive joins and every
P_{f}
preserves them.
A primary doctrine

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}

is
universal

if, for
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
in
\mathpzc{C}
, for a(ny) projection

\mathrm{pr}_{i}:A_{1}\times A_{2}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A_{i}
,
i=1,2
,
the functor
P_{\mathrm{pr}_{i}}:P(A_{i})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.26039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A_{1}\times A_{2})
has a right adjoint

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}\kern-2.58334pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{i}}
, and these satisfy the Beck-Chevalley condition:

for any pullback diagram

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}}
\textstyle{A}

with
\mathrm{pr}
a projection (hence also
\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}
a projection), for any

\beta
in
P(X)
, the canonical arrow

P_{f}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}\kern-2.58334pt_{\mathrm{pr}}(\beta)\leq\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}\kern-2.58334pt_{\mathrm{pr}^{\prime}}P_{f^{\prime}}(\beta)
in
P(A^{\prime})
is iso.

2.15 Definition
.

A
first-order doctrine
(f.o.d.) on
\mathpzc{C}
is an existential elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
which is also implicational, disjunctive and universal.

2.16 Examples
.

(a)
Consider a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
and the associated primary doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(a). If
\mathscr{L}
then the doctrine
LT
is first-order. In each fibre joins are given by disjunctions while the cartesian closure is provided by the implication. Right adjoints are given by universal quantification.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be a frame. Then it is a complete Heyting algebra. Hence the doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is first-order where in each fibre the Heyting algebra operations are computed point-wise and right adjoint are give by arbitrary infima (see
[
Pit02
]
for details).

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits.
The doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is first-order if and only if
\mathpzc{C}
is an Heyting category (or a logos) in the sense of
[
FS90
]
.

(d)
Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits. If
\mathpzc{C}
has finite coproducts and is weakly locally cartesian closed, then

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as in
2.2
-(d) is first-order. We shall se later how to generalise the description to the case in which
\mathpzc{C}
is assumed to have weak pullbacks.

We will often deal with different doctrines on the same base and in several situations one of these is
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
, thus we find it convenient to adopt a specific notation to distinguish operations between doctrines and in particular operations in
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
. We will use the following.

2.17 Notation
.

We write
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(f)
as
f^{*}
.
The left adjoint to
f^{*}
will be denoted by
\Sigma_{f}
. If
f^{*}
has a right adjoint this will be denoted by
\Pi_{f}
. The equality predicated over
A
will be denoted by
[\langle\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{A}\rangle]
. Binary meets in
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
will be denoted by
[f]\times_{A}[g]
while the top element over
A
will be denoted by
[\mathrm{id}_{A}]
. Joins will be denoted by
[f]+_{A}[g]
. The bottom element will be
0_{A}
. We will freely confuse a class with any of its representatives.

2.18 Definition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
a primary doctrine. An object
\Omega
in
\mathpzc{C}
is a
weak predicate classifier
if there is an element
\in_{1}
over
\Omega
such that for every
\phi
in
P(A)
there is a (not necessarily unique) morphism
\chi_{\phi}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
satisfying
P_{\chi_{\phi}}(\in_{1})=\phi
.

P
has a
strong predicate classifier
, or simply a
predicate classifier
if it has a weak predicate classifier and arrows of the form
\chi_{\phi}
are unique.

P
has
weak power objects
if for every
\mathpzc{C}
-object
A
there exists an object
\mathbb{P}{A}
in
\mathpzc{C}
and an object
\in_{A}
in
P(A\times\mathbb{P}{A})
such that for every
Y
and relation
\phi
in
P(A\times Y)
there is a (not necessarily unique) morphism
\chi_{\phi}:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathbb{P}{A}
satisfying
P_{\mathrm{id}_{A}\times\chi_{\phi}}(\in_{A})=\phi
.
P
has a
strong power object
if it has a weak power objects and arrows of the form
\chi_{\phi}
are unique.

Observe the following relation between power objects and predicate
classifiers. This is well known when
P
is
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
for a finite limit
category
\mathpzc{C}
and it can be proved analogously for a generic
P
.

2.19 Proposition
.

If
P
as (weak) power objects, then it has also a (weak) predicate
classifier (it suffices to choose
\mathbb{P}{1}
as
\Omega
).
Moreover, if the base
\mathpzc{C}
is weakly cartesian closed, then
P
has a
(weak) predicate classifier if and only if it has (weak) power objects, where

\mathbb{P}{A}
can be chosen as the weak exponential of
\Omega
to the power of

A
.

2.20 Examples
.

(a) The doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
built out of a theory

\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
has no predicate
classifiers.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be an inf-semilattice. The underlying set of
\mathpzc{H}
is
denoted
|\mathpzc{H}|
and is a strong predicate classifier of the functor

\mathcal{P}{H}
where
\in_{1}
is the identity
\mathrm{id}_{|\mathpzc{H}|}
.

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits. The doctrine

\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as a predicate classifier if and only if it
has a subobject classifier.

(d) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and weak pullbacks. If

\mathpzc{C}
has a weak predicate classifier if and only if it has a weak proof
classifier in the sense of
[
Men03
]
.

2.21 Definition
.

A primary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a tripos if
P
is a first-order doctrine with weak power objects.
P
is a
strong tripos
if it is a tripos
with strong power objects.

2.22 Examples
.

(a) The doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
built out of a theory

\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
is not a tripos as it
has no predicate classifiers.

(b) Let
\mathpzc{H}
be an inf-semilattice. The doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}
as in

2.2
is a tripos if and only if
\mathpzc{H}
is a frame.

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits. The doctrine

\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a strong tripos if and only if
\mathpzc{C}

is an elementary topos.

(d) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and pullbacks. If
\mathpzc{C}

has a weak predicate classifier if and only if it has a weak proof classifier
in the sense of
[
Men03
]
.

A proof of the following proposition is in
[
Pas16
]
.

2.23 Proposition
.

If the doctrine
P
is a tripos then
{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
is a tripos, too.

Elementary doctrines are the cloven Eq-fibrations of

[
Jac99
]
and, as explained in
loc.cit.
and
[
MR16
]
, there is a
deductive calculus associated to those which is that of the

\mathord{\wedge}\mathord{=}
-fragment over type theory with just a unit
type and a binary product type constructor. To fix notation, we will use the following.

2.24 Notation
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be elementary. Write

a_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,a_{k}:A_{k}\mid\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}\vdash\psi

in place of

\phi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\phi_{n}\leq\psi\quad\mbox{ in }P(A_{1}\times\ldots\times A_{k})

and call such an expression sequent. Note that, in line with
loc.cit.
,

\delta_{A}
in
P(A\times A)
will be written as
a:A,a^{\prime}:A\mid a\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a^{\prime}
. Also
write
a:A\mid\alpha\dashv\vdash\beta
to abbreviate

a:A\mid\alpha\vdash\beta
and
a:A\mid\beta\vdash\alpha
. Say that
\alpha
in
P(A)
is true over
A
if
\top_{A}\leq\alpha
. An element of
P(1)
will be called sentence. An arrow
r:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
will be called constant (of type
A
) and for
\alpha
in
P(A)
we write
\alpha(r)
in place of
P_{r}(\alpha)
. If
P
is existential and
a:A,x:X\mid\phi
,
i.e.

\phi
is in
P(A\times X)
, write
a:A\mid\exists_{x:X}\phi
in place of
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\phi
in
P(A)
. Similarly when
P
is first-order, for
\alpha,\beta
in
P(A)
and
\phi
in
P(A\times X)
write
a:A\mid\alpha\lor\beta
and
a:A\mid\alpha\Rightarrow\beta
and
a:A\mid\forall_{x:X}\phi
in place of
\alpha\lor\beta
and
\alpha\rightarrow\beta
and
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle A$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}}\kern-2.58334pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}\phi
in
P(A)
. If
P
is a tripos write
a:A,U:\mathbb{P}{A}\mid a\in_{A}U
in place of
\in_{A}
in
P(A\times\mathbb{P}{A})
.

From now on we feel free to employ this logical language in our proofs or definitions whenever we feel that readability is improved.

2.1
Comprehensions and strong monomorphisms

2.25 Definition
.

A primary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is said to have
weak comprehensions
if for every
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
\alpha
in
P(A)
there is an arrow
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
with
\top_{X}=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\alpha)
such that for every arrow
f:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
with
\top_{Y}\leq P_{f}(\alpha)
there is a (not necessarily unique) arrow
k:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
with
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}k=f
.
We shall say that comprehensions are
strong
if the mediating arrow
k
is the unique such arrow. We shall say that comprehensions are
full
if for every
A
and every
\alpha,\beta
over
A
it holds that
\alpha\leq\beta
if and only if
\top_{X}=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\beta)
.

It is an easy check that a doctrine
P
with weak comprehensions has strong comprehension if and only if arrows of the form are monic.

2.26 Examples
.

(a)
The doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
built out of a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language
\mathscr{L}
has no comprehensions.

(b) When
\mathpzc{H}
is an inf-semilattice, the doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}
has strong comprehensions. Given
\alpha
in
\mathcal{P}{H}(A)
the arrow
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is the inclusion of
\{a\in A\mid\alpha(a)=\top\}
into
A
. This comprehension is not full as one can see taking
\alpha^{\prime}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{H}
that agrees with
\alpha
only on those elements
a
of
A
such that
\alpha(a)=\top
. Then
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
have the same comprehension arrow, even if they are not necessarily equivalent in the fibre.

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits.
The doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as full strong comprehensions. The comprehension
\alpha
in
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
is any representative of
\alpha
.

(d)
Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and weak pullbacks. The doctrine
