\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
has full weak comprehensions. The comprehension
\alpha
in
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
is any representative of
\alpha
(that need not be monic).

An arrow of the form
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
will be often called comprehension arrow
of
\alpha
. We recall from
[
MR13a
]
the following definition.

2.27 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
has

comprehensive diagonals
if and only if diagonals in
\mathpzc{C}
are the
strong comprehension arrows of the corresponding fibred equalities,

i.e.

<id_{A},id_{A}>=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\delta_{A}}|\kern-2.5pt\}
.

Comprehensive diagonals were introduced originally in
[
MR13b
]

with the name of “comprehensive equalizers” since the following holds.

2.28 Proposition
.

Let
P
be an elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
. The following are
equivalent.

(i)

P
has
comprehensive diagonals
.

(ii)

P
is extensional as in def.
2.6
,
i.e.
for any

f,g:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
,
\top_{X}=P_{<f,g>}(\delta_{A})
if and only
f=g
.

Proof.

See
[
MR13b
, Proposition 4.6]
.
∎

2.29 Proposition
.

If
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary with comprehensive diagonals and
strong (weak) comprehensions, then
\mathpzc{C}
has (weak) finite limits.

Proof.

The equalizer of
f,g:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{<f,g>}(\delta_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
which is only
weak if
P
has only weak comprehensions.
∎

2.30 Proposition
.

Suppose
P
is elementary existential with weak comprehensions. Weak
comprehensions are full if and only if for every
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
it
holds
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})=\alpha
.

Proof.

Let
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}\dashv P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
, from

\top_{X}=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\alpha)
it follows
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})\leq\alpha
. Instead
\alpha\leq\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})
follows by fullness
from the adjunction unit

\top_{X}\leq P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X}))
.

Conversely, if
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})=\alpha
from

\top_{X}=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\beta)

it follows

\alpha=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\beta)\leq\beta
by the adjunction counit of

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}\dashv P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
.
∎

2.31 Proposition
.

If
P
has full weak comprehensions and comprehensive diagonals, then for
every weak pullback
fg=hk
one has
P_{f}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{g}P_{k}
.

Proof.

This is theorem 2.19 in
[
MPR17
]
.
∎

Suppose that
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.15967pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
has weak comprehensions. For every

f:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.85413pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
\alpha
in
P(B)
there is a commutative
square

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
\textstyle{m}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{B}

where
m
is the mediating arrow coming from the universal property of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
as
P_{f\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\alpha)=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(P_{f}(\alpha))=\top_{X}
.

2.32 Proposition
.

If
k:Z\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
and
h:Z\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
are such that
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}k=fh
, then
h
factors through
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}
. If
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is a strong comprehension, the square above is a weak pullback. If both
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
and
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}
are strong comprehensions the square is a pullback.

Proof.

Consider
k
and
h
such that
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}k=fh
. Then
P_{h}(P_{f}(\alpha))=P_{k}(P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\alpha))=\top_{Z}
. Weak universality of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}
guarantees the existence of
u:Z\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
with
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}u=h
. If
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is strong, then it is also monic. Hence
mu=k
if and only if
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}mu=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}k
, which is true as
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}mu=f\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}u=fh=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}k
. Finally if
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\alpha)}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is monic the mediating arrow
u
is necessarily unique.
∎

If
P
is elementary, we say that an arrow
f:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
of
\mathpzc{C}
is
{P}
-injective
if
P_{f\times f}(\delta_{A})=\delta_{X}
. While if
P
is also existential we say that
f
is
{P}
-surjective
if

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{f}(\top_{X})=\top_{A}
.

It is immediate to show that if
P
has comprehensive diagonals, if an arrow is
{P}
-injective
, then it is also monic and if an arrow is
{P}
-surjective
, then it is also epic.
Observe the following property about monics.

2.33 Proposition
.

Suppose
P
is an elementary doctrine with full weak comprehension and comprehensive diagonals on
\mathpzc{C}
. An arrow
m:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.6475pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.6475pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is
{P}
-injective
if and only if it is monic.

Proof.

If
m:X\rightarrow A
is
{P}
-injective
then it is clearly monic thanks to comprehensive diagonals. Conversely, if
m
is monic the square
<\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{A}>m=(m\times m)<\mathrm{id}_{X},\mathrm{id}_{X}>
is a pullback. Since
P
has comprehensive diagonals, and the equalizer of
m
with itself is

\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{<m,m>}(\delta_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
by prop.
2.29
, it follows that
<\mathrm{id}_{X},\mathrm{id}_{X}>=\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{<m,m>}(\delta_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
and since
<id_{X},id_{X}>=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\delta_{X}}|\kern-2.5pt\}

by fullness of comprehensions we conclude
P_{<m,m>}(\delta_{A})=\delta_{X}
,
i.e.

m
is
{P}
-injective
.
∎

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.6475pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.6475pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary existential doctrine. Using the language in
2.24
, a
F
in
P(A\times B)
is

total (or entire)

if
\exists_{b:B}F(a,b)
is true over
A

single-valued (or functional)

if
a:A,b:B,b^{\prime}:B\mid F(a,b)\wedge F(a,b^{\prime})\vdash b\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{B}}b^{\prime}

2.34 Definition
.

We say that
P
satisfies
the
rule of unique choice
(RUC) if for every total and single-valued
F
in
P(A\times B)
there is
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
such that
a:A,b:B\mid F(a,b)\dashv\vdash f(a)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{B}}b
.

2.35 Definition
.

We say that
P
satisfies
the
rule of choice
(RC) if for every total
F
in
P(A\times B)
there is
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
such that
a:A\mid\exists_{b:B}F(a,b)\dashv\vdash F(a,f(a))
.

2.36 Proposition
.

Suppose
P
is an existential m-variational doctrine on
\mathpzc{C}
. The following are equivalent.

(i)

Every arrow which is both
{P}
-injective
and
{P}
-surjective
is an isomorphism
the subobject doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
.

(ii)

P
satisfies
(RUC)
.

(iii)

P
is equivalent to the subobject doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
.

Proof.

To prove the equivalence between
(1)
and
(2)

we use the notation in
2.24
.
If
(1)
holds, take
F
in
P(A\times B)
and consider
\{\kern-2.5pt|{F}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A\times B
. If
F
is total and single-valued. Then
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\{\kern-2.5pt|{F}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.34196pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is

-injective
and
{P}

so it has an inverse

Doctrines with comprehensive diagonals and full weak comprehensions form the class of doctrines which we will mainly concerned with throughout this paper. In
[
MPR17
]
, taking the terminology from
[
Gra00
]
, we called them
variational
. While we called
m-variational doctrines
those variational doctrines in which comprehension is strong.
We aim at giving a characterisation of variational doctrines in
3.8
. To do this we first need some instrumental definitions and propositions.

Existential variational doctrines will be the main mathematical tool that we will employ throughout the whole paper. They form the category
EV
which is the full subcategory of
EExD
on those doctrines that are also existential. We denote by
EmV
the subcategory of
EV
on m-variational doctrines and on those morphisms of doctrine that preserves strong comprehension.
The adjoint situation described in
2.40
and in
2.9
compose to give the following

EED
\textstyle{\bot}
EmV

(1)

2.1.1
Factorization systems from doctrines

Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
is a category with weak pullbacks. A
right weak factorization system
is a pair
(\mathpzc{E},\mathpzc{R})
of classes of arrows of
\mathpzc{C}
such that

(i)

for every
f
in
\mathpzc{C}
there is
e
in
\mathpzc{E}
and
r
in
\mathpzc{R}
with
f=re

(ii)

for every commutative square
rf=ge
with
e
in
\mathpzc{E}
and
r
in
\mathpzc{R}
there is
k
with
rk=g
.

A right weak factorization system is stable if any weak pullback of an arrow of
\mathpzc{E}
is in
\mathpzc{E}
.
A proper factorisation system
(\mathpzc{E},\mathpzc{R})
in the sense of
[
FK72
]
is a
right weak factorization system such that every arrow in
\mathpzc{R}
is monic and every arrow in
\mathpzc{E}
is epic.
Every proper stable factorisation system
(\mathpzc{E},\mathpzc{R})
on a category
\mathpzc{C}
with
pullbacks gives rise to an existential m-variational doctrine

\mathpzc{R}{C}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
where
\mathpzc{R}{C}
is the sub-infsemilattice of

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
on those arrows represented by arrows in
\mathpzc{R}
, which are monic and
hence also in
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)

[
HJ03
]
. Analogously, to what shown in
loc.cit.
and observed in
[
MR16
]
) we can show that
category of existential elementary doctrines with full strong comprehensions is equivalent
to the category of proper stable factorisation systems.
More precisely,

2.41 Proposition
.

In every m-variational doctrine comprehension arrows and
{P}
-surjective
arrows form a factorization system. Moreover, the category of existential m-variational doctrines is equivalent to the category of proper stable factorisation systems where diagonals are in the right class.

2.2
The elementary quotient completion

We now recall the definition of
P
-equivalence relation
for any elementary
doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
and the related notion of
quotients

from
[
MR13a
]
.

2.42 Definition
.

A
P
-equivalence relation
\rho
over an object
A
of
\mathpzc{C}
is an element in
P(A\times A)
such that

(i)

\delta_{A}\leq\rho

(ii)

\rho=P_{<{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}>}(\rho)

(iii)

P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\rho)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\rho)\leq P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\rho)

where in (ii)
{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
are the first and the second
projection, while in (iii)
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
, with
i=1,2,3
, are projections from

A\times A\times A
to each of the factors.

When no confusion arises, we shall refer at
P
-equivalence relations simply as
equivalence relations, without specifying the doctrine
P
. Note that in
every elementary doctrine, fibred equalities are equivalence relations.

2.43 Examples
.

(a)
Recall from
2.2
-(a) the syntactic doctrine
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

built out of a theory
\mathscr{T}
over a first-order language

\mathscr{L}
. A
LT
-equivalence relation over
x
is a formula
\phi
of

\mathscr{L}
such that
\mathscr{T}\vdash\forall x\,\phi(x,x)
and

\mathscr{T}\vdash\forall xy\,(\,\phi(x,y)\rightarrow\phi(y,x)\,)
and

\mathscr{T}\vdash\forall xyz\,(\phi(x,y)\,\&\,\phi(y,z)\rightarrow\phi(x,z)\,)
.

(b) When
\mathpzc{H}
is an inf-semilattice, a
\mathcal{P}{H}
-equivalence relation over a set
A
is an
\mathpzc{H}
-valued ultra-pseudodistance on
A
after inverted the order,
i.e.
a function
\rho:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{H}
such that for all
a,a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime}
in
A
it is
\rho(a,a)=\top
and
\rho(a,a^{\prime})=\rho(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})
and
\rho(a,a^{\prime})\wedge\rho(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\leq\rho(a,a^{\prime\prime})
.

(c) Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits,
then
\rho
is a
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
-equivalence relation over
A
if and only if
\rho
is an equivalence relation of
\mathpzc{C}
in the usual categorical sense (see for example
[
MM92
]
).

(d)
Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and weak pullbacks, then
\rho
is a

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
-equivalence relation over
A
if and only if it is a pseudo-equivalence
relation of
\mathpzc{C}
in the sense of
[
Car95
]
.

2.44 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is said to have
quotients
if for every
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
equivalence relation
\rho
over
A
there exists a morphism
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
such
that
\rho\leq P_{q\times q}(\delta_{A/\rho})
and for every morphism
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y

such that
\rho\leq P_{f\times f}(\delta_{Y})
there exists a unique
h:A/\rho\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
with
hq=f
. Maps of the form
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
will be called

quotient arrow
of
\rho
. The quotient
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
is
effective
if
\rho=P_{q\times q}(\delta_{A/\rho})
.

2.45 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P
is said to have
stable quotients
if
for every pullback
qp=fh
, if
q
is a quotient arrow of
\rho
, then
h
is a quotient arrow of
P_{p\times p}(\rho)
.

2.46 Definition
.

Given an elementary doctrine

P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\longrightarrow{\textsf{POS}}
and
a
P
-equivalence relation
\rho
over
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
,
The inf-semilattice of
descent data

\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
is the sub-inf-semilattice of

P(A)
on those
\alpha
such that

P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\alpha)\wedge\rho\leq P_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\alpha),

where
\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
are the projections.

For
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
in
\mathpzc{C}
the
map
P_{f}:P(B)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P(A)
takes values in

\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{P_{f\times f}(\delta_{B})}
. We shall say that
f
is
of effective descent
if

P_{f}:P(B)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{P_{f\times f}(\delta_{B})}
is an isomorphism.
In particular this means that the functor
P_{f}
is of
effective descent type
as defined in
[
BW84
]
.

2.47 Definition
.

An elementary doctrine
P
is said to have
descent effective quotients
if
P
has stable
effective quotients and the quotient arrows are of effective descent.

2.48 Examples
.

(a)
In general doctrines of the form
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
do not have quotients.

(b) For
\mathpzc{H}
an inf-semilattice, the doctrine
\mathcal{P}{H}
has quotient. Let
\rho:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{H}
be a
\mathpzc{H}
-valued ultra-pseudodistance on
A
as in
2.43
-(b) and define on
A
the equivalence relation
a\sim a^{\prime}
generated by
\rho(a,a^{\prime})=\top
. Then canonical surjection
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\sim
is a quotient arrow. These quotients are not effective (unless
\rho
is the boolean equality on
A
) and hence not of effective descent.

(c) If
\mathpzc{C}
has finite limits,
then
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
has effective quotient if and only if
\mathpzc{C}
is exact. In this case quotients are of effective descent (this follows also from the more general situation of
2.46
in the following).

(d)
In general doctrines of the form
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
do not have quotients (unless
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
is equivalent to
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
for
\mathpzc{C}
exact).

2.49 Lemma
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
an elementary and existential doctrine and
q:A\rightarrow A/\rho
an effective descent arrow. Then, the inverse of
P_{q}:P(B)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
is the restriction of
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}
to
\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
and, hence, any
\phi
of
P(A)
is a descent data
if and only if
\phi=P_{q}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\phi
.

2.50 Remark
.

For elementary and existential doctrines
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
we could have simply define an effective quotient arrow
q:A\rightarrow A/\rho
of effective descent
if and only if
P_{q}
restricts to an isomorphisms toward its image in
P(A)
. Then, trivially an object
\phi
is in the image of
P_{q}
if and only if
\phi=P_{q}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\phi
,
which holds if and only if

\phi
is a descent data in the sense of definition
2.46
by Beck-Chevalley conditions applied to the description of
D_{q}
in remark 2.13
p.381 in
[
MR13b
]
.

2.51 Lemma
.

If
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary and existential, an effective quotient arrow
q:A\rightarrow A/\rho
is of effective descent if and only if the arrow

q
is
{P}
-surjective
,
i.e.

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\top_{A}=\top_{A/\rho}
.

Proof.

If
q
is of effective descent, then
P_{q}:P(A/\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
is an isomorphism with inverse the restriction of
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}
to
\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
by lemma
2.49
. Hence, for all descent data
\phi
, we have that

\phi=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}P_{q}\phi
from which
\top_{A/\rho}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\top_{A}
.
Conversely, if
q
is
{P}
-surjective
, observe, that for every
\psi
in
P(A/\rho)
, by Frobenius condition

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}P_{q}\psi=\psi\ \wedge\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\top_{B}=\psi
and hence
P_{q}
is an isomorphism towards
\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}
with inverse
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}
since
by lemma
2.49
we also know that every descent data satisfies
\phi=P_{q}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}\phi
.
∎

2.52 Proposition
.

The quotient arrows of an existential m-variational doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
with
stable effective quotients are of effective descent.

Proof.

By
2.41
each quotient arrow
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
can be factored as
q=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}g
where
g:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}C
is
{P}
-surjective
.
Since
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is monic, it is also
{P}
-injective
by
2.33
, whence
\rho=P_{q\times q}(\delta_{A})=P_{g\times g}P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.90417pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}\times\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.90417pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\delta_{A})=P_{g\times g}(\delta_{C})
. The universal property of quotients implies the existence of an arrow
s:A/\rho\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}C

with
g=sq
and hence
q=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}sq
, so
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}s=\mathrm{id}_{B}
as
q
is
epic. Since
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{q}(\top_{A})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is a monomorphism with a section it is an
isomorphism. Therefore
q
is isomorphic to
g
and hence
{P}
-surjective
. An application of
Lemma
2.51
concludes the proof.
∎

We recall from
[
MR13b
]
the construction called
elementary quotient completion
that freely adds descent effective quotients to any elementary doctrine.

2.53 Definition
.

Given an elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.45834pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
we call

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
the category whose objects are pairs
(A,\rho)
in which
A
is in
\mathpzc{C}
and
\rho
is an equivalence relation over
A
. An arrow
[f]:(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(B,\sigma)
is an equivalence class of arrows
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
in
\mathpzc{C}
such that
\rho\leq P_{f\times f}(\sigma)
, with respect to the equivalence
f\sim g
if and only if

\top_{A}=P_{<f,g>}(\sigma)

The category
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has finite products: if
(A,\rho)
and
(B,\sigma)
are objects of
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
, their product is

\textstyle{(A,\rho)}
\textstyle{(A\times B,\rho\boxtimes\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}]}
\textstyle{(B,\sigma)}

where
\rho\boxtimes\sigma=P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\rho)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{3},\mathrm{pr}_{4}>}(\sigma)
.
The elementary quotient completion of
P
is the doctrine
\widehat{P}:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
where

\widehat{P}(A,\rho)=\mathscr{D}\kern-1.29167pt\textit{es\kern 0.86108pt}_{\rho}\qquad\widehat{P}_{[f]}=P_{f}

It is proved in
[
MR13b
]
that the assignment on arrows does not depend on the choice of representatives. The doctrine
\widehat{P}
is elementary with
\delta_{(A,\rho)}=\rho
. It is immediate to see that
\widehat{P}
as stable descent effective quotient: if
\sigma
is an equivalence relation over
(A,\rho)
, then its quotient arrow is
[\mathrm{id}_{A}]:(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\sigma)
.
Moreover these quotients are stable.

2.54 Examples
.

(a)
The elementary quotient completion of doctrines of the form
LT:\mathpzc{V}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is connected to elimination of imaginaries as analysed in
[
EPR20
]
.

(b) Assuming choice, in the sense that epimorphism in
\mathpzc{Set}
split, the base of
\widehat{\mathcal{P}{H}}:\mathpzc{Set}_{\mathcal{P}{H}}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is equivalent to
{\textsf{UM}}{H}
, the category of
\mathpzc{H}
-valued ultrametric spaces. Indeed the functor that maps
f:(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(B,\sigma)
in
{\textsf{UM}}{H}
to
[f]:(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(B,\sigma)
in
\mathpzc{Set}_{\mathcal{P}{H}}
is full and faithful as Leibniz’s principle of identity of indiscernibles holds. For essential surjectivity, take
(A,\rho)
in
\mathpzc{Set}_{\mathcal{P}{H}}
and consider the quotient
(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A/\sim,\delta_{A/\sim})
where
\sim
is the equivalence relation described in
2.48
-(b). Any of its section represent an inverse in
\mathpzc{Set}_{\mathcal{P}{H}}
.

(d)
One the motivating examples for the study of the elementary quotient completion is given by doctrines of the form
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
where
\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and weak pullbacks. As proved in
[
MR13b
]
the doctrine
\widehat{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}
is
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}}
,
i.e.
the subobject doctrine of the exact completion
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
of
\mathpzc{C}
.

2.55 Remark
.

It is quite evident that the elementary structure plays no role in the
construction of
\widehat{P}
. We refer the reader to

[
Pas15
,
EPR20
]
for
a detailed analysis of the situation. In particular, there is shown also that
the elementary structure is necessary to embed
\mathpzc{C}
into
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.

The embedding is given by the functor
J:\mathpzc{C}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
that
assigns to each
f:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
the arrow
[f]:(X,\delta_{X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(Y,\delta_{Y})
. This
functor preserves binary products, and it is full; it is faithful exactly
when
P
has comprehensive diagonals (see
[
MR13b
]
). In this
case it is immediate to check that
P
is the change of base of
\widehat{P}
along

\nabla_{P}
.

Denote by
QEExD
the subcategory of
EExD
on those doctrines with effective descent quotients and on those arrows that preserves quotients,
i.e.
on those
(F,b)
from
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
to
R:\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
such that the action of
F
on
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
is
Fq:FA\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}FA/(b_{A\times A}\rho)
.

The following theorem is proved in
[
MR13b
,
MR13a
]
.

2.56 Proposition
.

There is a left biadjoint to the inclusion of
QEExD
into
EExD
which associates the doctrine
\widehat{P}:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
to an elementary
doctrine
P
.

2.57 Proposition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
an elementary
existential doctrine.

(i)

P
is implicational, respectively universal, if and only if
\widehat{P}

is so.

(ii)

P
is existential if and only if
\widehat{P}
is existential.

Proof.

The sufficient condition follows from

[
MR13b
, Proposition 6.7]
. The necessary condition is
immediate since
\widehat{P}
restricts to
P
on objects
(A,\delta_{A})
where

\delta_{A}
is the equality predicate relative to
P
.
∎

2.58 Proposition
.

If
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary with comprehensive diagonals and weak comprehensions, then
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has finite limits.

Proof.

If
P
has weak comprehensions, then
\widehat{P}
has strong comprehension. The claim follows by
2.29
.
∎

We also recall from
[
MR16
]

2.59 Proposition
.

An elementary and existential doctrine
P
satisfies
(RC)
if and only if its completion

\widehat{P}
satisfies
(RUC)
.

2.60 Remark
.

Let
{\textsf{EExD}}^{\mathrm{RUC}}
and
{\textsf{EExD}}^{\mathrm{RC}}
be the full subcategories of
EExD
on doctrines satisfying (RUC) and (RC) respectively and similarly

{\textsf{QEExD}}^{\mathrm{RUC}}
and
{\textsf{QEExD}}^{\mathrm{RC}}
are the full subcategories of
QEExD
on doctrines satisfying (RUC) and (RC) respectively. These categories fit in the following diagram of inclusions.

\textstyle{{\textsf{EExD}}^{\mathrm{RC}}\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dashv}
EExD
\textstyle{\dashv}
\textstyle{{\textsf{QEExD}}^{\mathrm{RUC}}\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
QEExD
\textstyle{\bot}

(2)

The vertical left adjoint in diagram (
2
) are given by the elementary quotient completion. The biadjoint to the inclusion of
{\textsf{QEExD}}^{\mathrm{RUC}}
into
QEExD
is the functor that maps
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.47583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.47583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
to
P_{F}:\mathpzc{EF}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.47583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.47583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
where
\mathpzc{C}_{F}
has the objects of
\mathpzc{C}
and an arrow
F:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.17029pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.17029pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
is a total and single-valued relation in
P(A\times B)
. The functor
P_{F}
maps
A
to
P_{F}(A)=P(A)
and given
\beta
in
P(B)
it is
P_{F}(\beta)
is the formula
a:A\mid\exists_{b:B}[F(a,b)\wedge\beta(b)]
over
A
(see
[
Pas16
]
).

It is worth noting that if we start with an elementary doctrine
P
without comprehensive diagonals and just with weak full comprehensions we can get
anyway a m-variational doctrine closed under effective quotients. Moreover, adding comprehensive diagonals to
P
before completing it with quotients
does produce the same doctrine as that obtained by completing
P
itself.

2.61 Theorem
.

Let
P
an elementary doctrine with weak full comprehensions.
Then
\widehat{P}
is a m-variational doctrine with stable effective quotients. Moreover, the doctrine
\widehat{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
is equivalent to
\widehat{P}
.

3
Topology on a doctrine

Consider the following two diagrams in
PD
.

\textstyle{{\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Id}_{\mathpzc{C}}}
POS
\textstyle{{\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{j}
\textstyle{\kern-1.72218pt\cdot}

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P}
\textstyle{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}^{op}}
POS
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R}
\textstyle{l{}\kern 2.15277pt\cdot\kern-2.15277pt}
\textstyle{\kern-2.15277pt\cdot\kern 2.15277ptr}
\textstyle{\dashv\ }

where the adjointness symbols in the diagram on the right means that for every
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
\alpha
in
P(A)
and every
\beta
in
R(A)
the inequalities
lr\alpha\leq\alpha
and
\beta\leq rl\beta
both hold.

3.1 Definition
.

Given two primary doctrines
P,R

We say that
P
and and
R
form an
adjoint-retraction pair
and we write
P\lhd R
if they fit in a diagram as the one on the right and moreover
lr=\mathrm{id}_{P}
.

3.2 Definition
.

Let
P
a primary doctrine.
An endomorphism of primary doctrine of the form
(\mathrm{id}_{C},j):P\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.65974pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.65974pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}P
is called a
topology on
P

if
j
is extensive and idempotent,
i.e.
for every
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and every
\alpha
in
P(A)
it holds
\alpha\leq j_{A}\alpha
and
j_{A}j_{A}\alpha=j_{A}\alpha
. An element
\alpha
in
P(A)
is
j
-closed
if
\alpha=j_{A}(\alpha)
.

Every topology
j
on
P
determines a doctrine
P_{j}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.96529pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 28.96529pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
as follows:

3.3 Definition
.

If
j
is a topology on the primary doctrine
P
, we call
doctrine of
j
-closed element of
P
the doctrine
P_{j}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.96529pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 28.96529pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

where
P_{j}(A)
is the sub-infsemilattice of
P(A)
on those
\alpha
such that
j_{A}\alpha=\alpha
.

The following is proved in
[
Pas16
]
; remarks in
[
MPR19
]
also useful.

3.4 Proposition
.

If
P\lhd R
then there is

L:\mathpzc{Q}_{R}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.65974pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.65974pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
and
