[q],[p]:(W,\delta_{W})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\delta_{X})
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
. Universality of
(W,\delta_{W})
ensures that
[q]=[p]
,
i.e.

\top_{W}\leq P_{<q,p>}(\delta_{X})
, whence
q=p
in
\mathpzc{C}
as diagonals are comprehensive.
∎

6.3 Proposition
.

Suppose
P
is a variational doctrine and
f:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
,
g:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
are arrows of
\mathpzc{C}
. Suppose also that
\rho
and
\sigma
are
P
-equivalence relations over
X
and
Y
respectively such that
\rho\leq P_{f\times f}(\delta_{A})
and
\sigma\leq P_{g\times g}(\delta_{A})
. Consider two arrows
k:P\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
and
h:P\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.42989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
and the following two squares

\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{h}
\textstyle{k}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g}
\textstyle{A}

\textstyle{(S,P_{h\times h}(\sigma)\wedge P_{k\times k}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[h]}
\textstyle{[k]}
\textstyle{(X,\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f]}
\textstyle{(Y,\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[g]}
\textstyle{(A,\delta_{A})}

If the left square is a weak pullback in
\mathpzc{C}
, the right square is a pullback in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
.

Proof.

Take any two arrows
[a]:(C,\gamma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\rho)
and
[b]:(C,\gamma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(Y,\sigma)
with
[f][a]=[g][b]
. That is to say
\top_{C}=P_{fa\times gb}(\delta_{A})
. Since diagonals are comprehensive we have
fa=gb
. Hence there is
u:C\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.72804pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}S
with
ku=a
and
hu=b
. Since
\gamma\leq P_{a\times a}(\rho)=P_{u\times u}P_{k\times k}(\rho)
and
\gamma\leq P_{b\times b}(\rho)=P_{u\times u}P_{h\times h}(\sigma)
one has
\gamma\leq P_{u\times u}(P_{h\times h}(\sigma)\wedge P_{k\times k}(\rho))
, showing that
[u]
is an arrow in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
, thus also
[k][u]=[a]
and
[h][u]=[b]
. If
[u^{\prime}]
is such that
[k][u^{\prime}]=[a]
and
[h][u^{\prime}]=[b]
, then also
[k][u^{\prime}]=[k][u]
and
[h][u^{\prime}]=[h][u]
,
i.e.

\top_{C}\leq P_{u\times u^{\prime}}P_{k\times k}(\rho)\qquad\top_{C}\leq P_{u\times u^{\prime}}P_{h\times h}(\sigma)

whence
\top_{C}\leq P_{u\times u^{\prime}}(P_{h\times h}(\sigma)\wedge P_{k\times k}(\rho))
showing that
[u]=[u^{\prime}]
.
∎

6.4 Proposition
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.03358pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 88.03358pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a variational doctrine and consider two squares of the form

\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{h}
\textstyle{k}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g}
\textstyle{A}

\textstyle{(S,\theta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[h]}
\textstyle{[k]}
\textstyle{(X,\delta_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f]}
\textstyle{(Y,\delta_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[g]}
\textstyle{(A,\delta_{A})}

If the right square is a pullback in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
, the left square is a weak pullback in
\mathpzc{C}

Proof.

Suppose
a:C\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
and
b:C\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
are such that
fa=gb
. Then
[a]:(C,\delta_{C})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\delta_{A})
and
[b]:(C,\delta_{A})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(Y,\delta_{Y})
are such that
[f][a]=[g][b]
. So there is
[u]
with
[k][u]=[a]
and
[h][u]=[b]
,
i.e.

\top_{C}=P_{<ku,a>}(\delta_{X})\qquad\top_{C}=P_{<hu,b>}(\delta_{Y})

Since diagonals are comprehensive,
u
is such that
ku=a
and
hu=b
in
\mathpzc{C}
.
∎

6.1
Local cartesian closure

We introduce some technical notions which will be used in
the proof of the characterisation Theorem
6.32
.

6.5 Definition
.

Let
\mathpzc{C}
be
a category with finite products and let
J:\mathpzc{D}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.13052pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{C}

be an inclusion of a subcategory in it. We say that an object
X
is

weakly exponentiable relative to

\mathpzc{D}
if the functor

X\times(-):\mathpzc{D}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.43607pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.43607pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{C}

is a weak left adjoint, in the sense of
[
Kai71
]
: for every
object
Y
in
\mathpzc{C}
there are an object
W
in
\mathpzc{D}
and an arrow

\textstyle{X\times J(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ev}}
\textstyle{Y}

in
\mathpzc{C}
such that for every
D
in
\mathpzc{D}
and every arrow

f:X\times J(D)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.98256pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.98256pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X\times J(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{id}_{X}\times J\left(\widehat{f}\right)}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{f}}
\textstyle{X\times J(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ev}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{W}

where the dotted arrow indicates that the condition need not determine it
uniquely.

6.6 Remark
.

The condition of weak left adjoint in Definition
6.5
provides a
family of surjective functions

\textstyle{\mathpzc{D}(D,W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{C}(X\times J(D),Y)}

natural in
D
.

We shall be interested in weak relative exponentiability in slice categories
of the form
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
. They shall involve a specific kind of
objects which will be introduced in Definition
6.8
.

6.7 Remark
.

Consider a category
\mathpzc{C}
with finite products. An object
Y
is weakly
exponentiable in the usual sense if (and only if) it is weakly exponentiable
relative to
\mathpzc{C}
. So
\mathpzc{C}
is weakly cartesian closed if and only if every
object is weakly exponentiable relative to
\mathpzc{C}
.

6.8 Definition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.6806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.6806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary doctrine, and let
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
be
its elementary quotient completion. An arrow in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
of the form

[f]:(X,\delta_{X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta_{A})
is called a

dependent
P
-projective
.
We write as
\mathpzc{D}_{A}
the full sucategory of
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
on the
dependent
P
-projectives in it.

In case
\mathpzc{C}
has (strong) pullbacks, local cartesian closure suffices to
show that the doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
is universal. In the weak case this
need not happen, and motivates the following definition.

6.9 Definition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.6806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.6806pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary doctrine with comprehensive
diagonals and weak full comprehension, then its base
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks and
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
has
pullbacks by Proposition
2.29
and
3.14
.
We say that
P
is
slice-wise weakly cartesian closed
when the following conditions are
satisfied:

(i)

the doctrine
P
is implicational and universal;

(ii)

for every object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
, each dependent
P
-projective is weakly
exponentiable in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
relative to
\mathpzc{D}_{A}
.

6.10 Remark
.

It may be useful to expand condition (ii) taking advantage of the full
embedding
J:\mathpzc{C}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
introduced in Remark
2.55
—so, in
particular,
JA=(A,\delta_{A})
. Given objects
Jf:JX\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}JA
and

[g]:(Y,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.37506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}JA
in the slice category
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/JA
, there is a
diagram of arrows in
\mathpzc{C}

\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ev}}
\textstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{w}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f}
\textstyle{A}

where the inner square is a weak pullback. The arrow
\mathrm{ev}:S\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.71524pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.71524pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is the
representative of an arrow

6.11 Remark
.

In
[
Cio23
]
, it is shown that a category

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
is an example of elementary quotient completion of a
suitable biased elementary doctrine for which dependent
P
-projectives

[f]:(X,\delta_{X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.11237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.11237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta_{A})
are covering projectives.

6.12 Definition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary doctrine with comprehensive
diagonals whose base
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks.
We say that
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is
slice-wise exponentiable on dependent projectives
if the following conditions are
satisfied.

(i)

The doctrine
P
is implicational and universal;

(ii)

For every object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
, each dependent
P
-projective is exponentiable in

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
.

6.13 Remark
.

In his Ph.D. thesis
[
Cio22
]
, Cipriano Jr. Cioffo introduced the
notion of
extensional exponential
, which is equivalent to the universal
property in remark
6.10
. In the case of a variational doctrine it
coincides with that of extensional exponential given by Jacopo
Emmenegger in
[
Emm20
]
.

Next we note a connection between the fibres
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
of the
variational doctrine on
\mathpzc{C}
and
\Psi_{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}(A,\delta_{A})
of
that on the elementary quotient completion
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.

6.14 Proposition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary doctrine with comprehensive
diagonals, and let
A
be an object in
\mathpzc{C}
. The functor

\nabla_{A}:\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Psi_{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}(A,\delta_{A})

induced by the functor
J:\mathpzc{C}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.41791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
of
Remark
2.55
is a full coreflective embedding.

Proof.

The functor
\nabla_{A}
is full thanks to comprehensive diagonals.
The right adjoint is

\textstyle{\Psi_{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}(A,\delta_{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{A}}
\textstyle{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)}
\textstyle{[f:(B,\sigma)\rightarrow(A,\delta_{A})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f:B\rightarrow A]}

6.15 Remark
.

Note that, without an assumption about existence of weak pullbacks in
\mathpzc{C}
,
the assignment
A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)
does not extend to a

does not have that hypothesis among the
assumptions because one is hard put to prove that the family of functors

But it is easy to prove that, if an arrow

in

\mathpzc{C}
is such that pullback of any arrow
g:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.76385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 10.76385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
along
f
exists in

\mathpzc{C}
, then the reindexing functors commute with
\nabla
as in the

\textstyle{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

6.16 Lemma
.

Suppose that
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary with comprehensive
diagonals, and
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks. If
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is locally
cartesian closed, then

(i)

the variational doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

is universal and implicational;

(ii)

if moreover
P
is existential, then
P
is a hyperdoctrine.

Proof.

Note first of all that, since
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is locally cartesian closed,
the variational doctrine
\Psi_{{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}}
is universal and
implicational. Write as
\forall^{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}_{\mathrm{pr}{}}
the right
adjoint to reindexing along a projection
\mathrm{pr}{}:D\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
.

(i) Since pullbacks along a projection always exist in
\mathpzc{C}
,
thanks to Proposition
6.14
, the
reindexing functor
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}(f)
along any
projection
f:D\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
has a right adjoint computed coreflecting
the restiction of
\forall^{\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}}_{f}
along
\nabla_{A}
. The
Beck–Chevalley condition for the universal quantification is satisfied since
any reindexing of the doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
has a left adjoint.

(ii) By Proposition
2.57
,
\widehat{P}
is existential since

P
is existential. Since
\widehat{P}
admits full strong comprehension, by the
previous point (i)
and Proposition
2.39
it follows that
\widehat{P}
is universal and
implicational. Applying again Proposition
2.57
yields that

P
is universal and implicational.
∎

6.17 Lemma
.

Suppose that
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.13026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary with
comprehensive diagonals, and that
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks.
If
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is locally cartesian closed, then
for every object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
, a dependent
P
-projective over
A
is
weakly exponentiable relative to
\mathpzc{D}_{A}
.

Proof.

Let
[f]:(X,\delta_{X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta_{A})
be a dependent
P
-projective and

g:(Y,\tau)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.82472pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta_{A})
any object in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.
Consider the following diagram in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}

\textstyle{(S^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[q_{2}]}
\textstyle{[q_{1}]}
\textstyle{(W,\delta_{W})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{id}_{W}]}
\textstyle{(S,\theta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{ev}]}

\textstyle{[p_{2}]}
\textstyle{[p_{1}]}
\textstyle{(W,\xi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[g]^{[f]}}
\textstyle{(Y,\tau)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[g]}
\textstyle{(X,\delta_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f]}
\textstyle{(A,\delta_{A})}

where the two squares are pullbacks and
[\mathrm{ev}]:(S,\theta)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(Y,\tau)

is the universal arrow of the exponential. Fix a representative
w
of the
equivalence class
[g]^{[f]}
. Then
w:(W,\delta_{W})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta)
together with

[\mathrm{ev}q_{2}]:(S^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.62045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(Y,\tau)
is clearly a weak exponential of

[f]
over
[g]
relative to the full subcategory
\mathpzc{D}_{A}
on the

P
-dependent projectives.
∎

6.18 Proposition
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.926pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.926pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is elementary with comprehensive diagonals, and

\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks. Suppose also that
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is locally
cartesian closed. If
P
is either universal or existential, then
P
is
slice-wise weakly cartesian closed.

Proof.

After Lemma
6.17
, one needs only to invoke Lemma
6.16
to
get that, in case
P
is existential,
P
is also universal and implicational.
∎

We now aim at proving a partial converse to Proposition
6.18
, as we
shall replace the assumption of weak pullbacks in
\mathpzc{C}
with that of the
doctrine
P
admitting full weak comprehension—we
shall consider only the case when the elementary doctrine
P
is universal and implicational because of
6.16
(ii).
To that purpose we produce an equivalent presentation of objects of

\mathpzc{C}/A
, giving an algebraic presentation in line with the
characterisation in
[
Mai09
, Proposition 4.12]
.
For sake of semplicity we introduce some explicit notation for certain arrows
related to constructions in the base
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
of the elementary quotient
completion.

6.19 Remark
.

in the internal logic of the doctrine

.

6.21 Proposition
.

In the notations of Remark
6.20
,
the following diagram

\textstyle{(B,\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f]}
\textstyle{[k]}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{id}_{B}]}
\textstyle{(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{id}_{X}]}
\textstyle{[f_{\rho}]}
\textstyle{[f^{\sigma}]}
\textstyle{(A,\rho)}

commutes in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.

Proof.

To complete the proof after Remark
6.20
, one must show that

[k]:(B,\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)
, and

[k][f^{\sigma}]=[\mathrm{id}_{X}]:(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)
.

The simple argument to see that
[k]:(B,\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)

performed in the internal logic is as follows:

x:B,x^{\prime}:B\mid\sigma(x,x^{\prime})\vdash\rho(f_{\rho}(k(x)),f_{\rho}(k(x^{\prime})))

because
f_{\rho}k=f
and
[f]:(B,\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.2216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)
, and

x:B,x^{\prime}:B\mid\sigma(x,x^{\prime})\vdash\sigma(f^{\sigma}(k(x)),f^{\sigma}(k(x^{\prime})))

because
f^{\sigma}k=\mathrm{id}_{B}
.
To prove that
[k][f^{\sigma}]=[\mathrm{id}_{X}]
, again in the internal logic, the
simple checks are as follows: recalling (
4
) and (
3
),
one sees that

\begin{array}[]{r@{{}\vdash{}}l}x:X&\rho(ff^{\sigma}(x),f_{\rho}(x))\\[4.30554pt]
&\rho(f_{\rho}(kf^{\sigma}(x)),f_{\rho}(x))\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{r@{{}\vdash{}}l}x:X&\sigma(f^{\sigma}(x),f^{\sigma}(x))\\[4.30554pt]
&\sigma(f^{\sigma}(kf^{\sigma}(x)),f^{\sigma}(x))\end{array}

So conjoining the two conclusions gives the statement.
∎

6.22 Remark
.

Proposition
6.21
shows that

[f^{\sigma}]:[f_{\rho}]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.50002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern-2.77779pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{3072}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces[f]
in the slice category
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\rho)
.
Note, though, that
[f]
and
[f_{\rho}]
need
not factor through each other in
\mathpzc{C}
. Indeed, Remark
6.20

shows that
f
factors through
f_{\rho}
in
\mathpzc{C}
, but nothing guarantees
the other factorisation may occur.
Since
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
is a category with finite limits, one can see
that
[f_{\rho}]
is isomorphic to
\Sigma_{[\mathrm{id}_{A}]}[\mathrm{id}_{A}]^{\ast}([f])
, where

\Sigma_{[\mathrm{id}_{A}]}
denotes the left adjoint to the pullback functor

[\mathrm{id}_{A}]^{\ast}:\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
along the map

[\mathrm{id}_{A}]:(A,\delta_{A})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.76385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 10.76385pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)
.

The following is the fundamental step toward the proof of the main result. It
takes advantage of the iso
[f^{\sigma}]:[f_{\rho}]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.50002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern-2.77779pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{3072}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 12.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces[f]
in the slice
category
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\rho)
to compute explicitly any product of
[f]
in
the slice category starting from a product in the slice category

\mathpzc{C}/A
.
Like before, we employ the notation introduced in
6.19
.

6.23 Lemma
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an elementary existential doctrine with
comprehensive diagonals and admitting weak comprehension.
Consider two arrows

\textstyle{(W,\theta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[q]}
\textstyle{(B,\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f]}
\textstyle{(A,\rho)}

in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
, and consider the following diagrams

\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
3
\textstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f_{\rho}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{q}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{\rho}}
\textstyle{A}

\textstyle{(Z,P_{q^{\prime}\times q^{\prime}}(\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\wedge P_{f_{\rho}^{\prime}\times f_{\rho}^{\prime}}(\theta))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
4
\textstyle{[q^{\prime}]}
\textstyle{[f_{\rho}^{\prime}]}
\textstyle{(W,\theta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[q]}
\textstyle{(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f_{\rho}]}
\textstyle{(A,\rho).}

If
3
is a weak pullback in
\mathpzc{C}
, then
4
is a
pullback in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.

Proof.

We write
\zeta
for the
P
-equivalence relation

P_{q^{\prime}\times q^{\prime}}(\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\wedge P_{f_{\rho}^{\prime}\times f_{\rho}^{\prime}}(\theta)
on
Z
.
Clearly, if diagram
3
commutes in
\mathpzc{C}
, then so does

4
in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.
Consider a commutative diagram

\textstyle{(C,\gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[h]}
\textstyle{[\ell]}
\textstyle{(Z,\zeta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[q^{\prime}]}
\textstyle{[f_{\rho}^{\prime}]}
\textstyle{(W,\theta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[q]}
\textstyle{(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[f_{\rho}]}
\textstyle{(A,\rho)}

(5)

in
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
. So, in the internal logic of
P
, we have that

(a)

x,x^{\prime}:C\mid\gamma(x,x^{\prime})\vdash\rho(f_{\rho}(h(x)),f_{\rho}(h(x^{\prime})))
;

(b)

x,x^{\prime}:C\mid\gamma(x,x^{\prime})\vdash\sigma(f^{\sigma}(h(x)),f^{\sigma}(h(x^{\prime})))
;

(c)

x,x^{\prime}:C\mid\gamma(x,x^{\prime})\vdash\theta(\ell(x),\ell(x^{\prime}))
;

(d)

x:C\vdash\rho(f_{\rho}h(x),q\ell(x))
.

Recall that
[f_{\rho}]=[ff^{\sigma}]
by Proposition
6.21
. So

x:C\vdash\rho(ff^{\sigma}h(x),q\ell(x)).

Hence weak universality of
c_{\rho,f}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.51929pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.51929pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B\times A
produces a filler in

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{<h,\ell>}
\textstyle{j}
\textstyle{h}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{c_{\rho,f}}
\textstyle{<ff^{\sigma},f_{\rho}>}
\textstyle{f^{\sigma}}

\textstyle{X\times W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{\sigma}\times q}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}1^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B\times A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f\times\mathrm{id}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{pr}1}
\textstyle{A\times A}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{\sigma}}
\textstyle{B}

which shows that
[j]:(C,\gamma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.73079pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.73079pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(X,\rho\otimes_{f}\sigma)
and it is equal
to
[h]
. But also that the diagram

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j}
\textstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{q}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{\rho}}
\textstyle{A}

commutes in

. Therefore, since

is a weak pullback, there is
an arrow

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j}
\textstyle{m}
\textstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f_{\rho}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{q}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{\rho}}
\textstyle{A}

Thanks to the definition of the
P
-equivalence relation
\zeta
, it is
immediate to prove that that gives a unique arrow filling in the diagram
(
5
).
∎

6.24 Remark
.

It is possible to derive a moral from Lemma
6.23
. Even though there
are only weak pullbacks in
\mathpzc{C}
, each object in a slice category of

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
may be replaced by an isomorphic copy on which (strong) pullbacks
can be computed
as if
pullbacks were strong also in
\mathpzc{C}
.

As obscure as that moral may be, it is going to be employed in the
construction of exponentials in each slice category of
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}
.

We approach the main theorem of the section by first introducing the explicit
construction of an exponential in the slice
\mathpzc{Q}\,_{P}/(A,\rho)
; the
following remark presents the first steps of that construction by producing
the relevant
P
-equivalence relation to be used then in the proof of
Theorem
6.32
.

6.25 Remark
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.53535pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.53535pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be a fixed slice-wise weakly cartesian closed doctrine
which admit weak comprehension.
Let
[f]:(B,\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.22981pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 25.22981pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)

6.26 Lemma
.

Suppose

on
\mathpzc{C}
is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed, then for every

the slice

∎

After
6.26
it remains to prove that every slice of
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is cartesian closed. Proposition
6.26
says that for every
(A,\delta_{A})
and every
[f]
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
there is a right adjoint to the functor
-\times_{(A,\delta_{A})}[f]:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
. We aim at proving that these right adjoints exist for all
(A,\rho)
.

To this purpose we follow the line of the proof in
[
Emm20
]
by relying on the existence of right adjoints as a consequence of one of Barr’s tripability theorems in
[
BW84
]
.

We first need some instrumental propositions.

6.27 Proposition
.

In a m-variational doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
with stable effective quotients each quotient arrow is the coequalizer of its kernel pairs
and hence it is a regular epimorphism. Moreover
\mathpzc{C}
is regular.

Proof.

In
[
MR13b
]
it is proved that the kernel pairs of each map in
\mathpzc{C}
has a coequalizer which is an effective quotient arrow and hence
\mathpzc{C}
is regular.
∎

6.28 Corollary
.

In a m-variational doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
with stable effective quotients each coequalizer is a quotient arrow and it is stable under pullback.

Proof.

Every coequalizer
e:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
is isomorphic to
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/P_{e\times e}(\delta_{B})
.
∎

6.29 Proposition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be an existential variational doctrine and

\widehat{P}:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.66115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be its elementary quotient completion.
Then,
for every quotient arrow
q:(A,\delta_{A})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}

the functor
q^{\ast}:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A/\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
is monadic.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that for exact categories given in
[
JM95
]
.
Alternatively, just observe that
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is regular and that any quotient map is a regular epimorphism by lemma
6.27
. Therefore it is well known that
q^{\ast}
is of descent type in the sense of
[
BW84
]
.

Moreover, any map
f:(B,\tau)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)
is isomorphic over
(A,\rho)

to
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{<id,f>}(\rho)}|\kern-2.5pt\}:(X,\,P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\rho)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\tau))\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)

which comes from a descent datum represented by
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{<id,f>}(\rho)}|\kern-2.5pt\}:(X,\delta_{X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.3556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\delta_{A})

with the obvious action.
∎

Theorem
6.29
actually holds even in the more general situation of a generic existential variational doctrine with effective stable quotients with the same proof.

We recall from theorem 3.7.2 in
[
BW84
]
.

6.30 Proposition
.

In a situation like the following

\textstyle{\mathpzc{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W}
\textstyle{U}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{B^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathpzc{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}}

where
F\dashv U
and
F^{\prime}\dashv U^{\prime}
, if furthermore

(i)

WF
is naturally isomorphic to
F^{\prime}
,

(ii)

U
is monadic, and

(iii)

W
preserves co-equalizers of
U
-contractible pairs,

then
W
has a right adjoint.

We apply this theorem to our context as follows.

6.31 Proposition
.

If an m-variational elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{D}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.22218pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 28.22218pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
has stable effective quotients, then for every quotient arrow
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.91664pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.91664pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
, if the slice
\mathpzc{D}/A
is cartesian closed also the slice
\mathpzc{D}/(A/\rho)
is cartesian closed.

Proof.

The cartesian closure of
\mathpzc{D}/A
ensures that for every
\alpha
in
\mathpzc{D}/(A/\rho)
the functor
-\times_{A}q^{*}\alpha:\mathpzc{D}/A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.91664pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.91664pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{D}/A
has a right adjoint
R_{\alpha}
. Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathpzc{D}/(A/\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{-\times_{(A/\rho)}\alpha}
\textstyle{q^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{D}/(A/\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{\alpha}q^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{D}/A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{q}}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{q}(-\times_{A}q^{*}\alpha)}

Since
\Sigma_{q}\dashv q^{*}
and
\Sigma_{q}(-\times_{A}q^{*}\alpha)\dashv R_{\alpha}q^{*}
(by composition of adjoints), the claim is proved if we show that this situation meets the three conditions in proposition
6.30
,

Condition 1 holds since, for any
\beta
in
\mathpzc{C}/A
, Frobenius reciprocity precisely says that
\Sigma_{q}(\beta)\times_{(A/\rho)}\alpha
is isomorphic to
\Sigma_{q}(\beta\times_{A}q^{*}\alpha)
.

Condition 2 holds since every quotient arrow
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\rho
induces a monadic functor

q^{\ast}
by
6.29
.

Condition 3 is fullfiled as well, since in
\mathpzc{C}/(A/\rho)
coequalizers of
q^{\ast}
-contractible pairs are preserved by
-\times_{A}q^{*}\alpha
thanks to stability of coequalizers in corollary
6.28
.
∎

Now we are ready to prove the main theorem of the section.

6.32 Theorem
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is an
elementary existential variational doctrine.
The following are equivalent:

(i)

the base
\mathpzc{C}
is
P
-slice-wise weakly cartesian closed;

(ii)

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is slice-wise exponentiable on dependent projectives;

(iii)

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is locally cartesian closed.

Proof.

3.
\Rightarrow
2. Condition (ii) of the definition of slice-wise exponentiable on dependent projectives follows immediately, while condition (i) follows from prop.
6.16
.

3.
\Rightarrow
1. follows from lemma
6.18
and lemma
2.29
.

To show 1.
\Rightarrow
2. first note that
P
is universal and implicational by lemma
2.39
. Therefore, we can apply lemma
6.26
to deduce that every slice
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}/(A,\delta_{A})
is cartesian closed.

2.
\Rightarrow
3. It follows from the fact that, for every object
(A,\rho)
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
the arrow
[\mathrm{id}_{A}]:(A,\delta_{A})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\rho)
is a quotient arrow. The claim follows by prop
6.31
when
\mathpzc{D}
there is
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
.
∎

Recall that the exact completion of a category with binary products and weak pullbacks is an instance of the elementary quotient completion. More specifically
\widehat{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}\simeq\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}}
, which has as corollary
\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}\equiv\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
.

An important instance of Theorem
6.32
is when the doctrine
P
is the variational doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
.
In this case, we deduce that
\mathpzc{C}
is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed if and only if

\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is locally cartesian. Hence we recover the characterization in

[
CR00
]
and
[
Emm20
]
supposing
\mathpzc{C}
with finite products and weak equalizers.

6.33 Corollary
.

Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
a category with finite
products and weak equalizers. Then
\mathpzc{C}
is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed with respect to the weak subobject doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
if and only if
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is
locally cartesian closed.

Proof.

It follows from
6.32
when applied to
the doctrine functor of
variations

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

knowing that
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is equivalent to

\mathpzc{Q}\,_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}
, that
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
is elementary existential, and
it admits full weak comprehension and has comprehensive diagonals, since

\mathpzc{C}
has finite products and weak pullbacks.
∎

6.34 Remark
.

The proof of theorem
6.32
can be considered a generalization
of Carboni and Rosolini’s characterization of locally cartesian closed exact
completions
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
in
[
CR00
]

only
in the case
the category has finite products. It can be generalized to the case of weak
finite products in
[
Cio22
]
.
Related proofs of locally cartesian closure for the elementary quotient
completion of a syntactic category out of specific type theories are in

[
Pal19
]
and in
[
Mai09
]
.

6.2
Finite disjoint coproducts

In this section we establish the necessary and sufficient conditions under which an elementary quotient completion has stable finite coproducts.

We are interested in studying those elementary doctrines with comprehensive diagonals whose elementary quotient completion has coproducts.
After
6.2
we know that if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has coproducts then
