P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.1823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
must have coproducts.

For the rest of the section, unless specified otherwise,
P
is a variational f.o.d. on a category
\mathpzc{C}
with binary distributive coproducts.

6.35 Proposition
.

Canonical injections are
{P}
-injective
,
i.e.

P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B})=\delta_{A}
and
P_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})=\delta_{B}
.

Proof.

The idea of the proof is simple when formulate in the internal language of doctrines. Given an element
a:A
then we can define a projection
\overline{p}:A+B\rightarrow A
as follows

\overline{p_{a}}(z)\equiv\begin{cases}w\mbox{ if }z=i_{A}(w)\\
a\mbox{ if }z=i_{B}(w^{\prime})\\
\end{cases}

Hence if
i_{A}(x)=_{A+B}i_{A}(y)
then
x=_{A}\overline{p_{x}}(i_{A}(x))=_{A}\overline{p_{x}}(i_{A}(y))=_{A}y
.

We, now, give its algebraic version, whose calculations are more involved.

Suppose
i_{A}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.87675pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
is a canonical injection and consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{A\times A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{A}}
\textstyle{i_{A\times A}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}}
\textstyle{(A\times A)+(A\times B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]}
\textstyle{A\times B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{B}}
\textstyle{i_{A\times B}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}}
\textstyle{A\times(A+B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j}

where
j
is the isomorphism that comes from distributivity. Denote by
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
(
i=1,2,3,4,
) the projections from
Z=A\times(A+B)\times A\times(A+B)
. By Beck-Chevalley conditions on equality (see
[
MR13b
]
) one has both

\delta_{Z}=P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\delta_{A})\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{4}>}(\delta_{A+B})

\delta_{Z}\leq P_{j\times j}P_{[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]\times[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]}(\delta_{A})

Evaluating both sides on
P_{e}
for

e=<{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},i_{A}{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},i_{A}{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}>:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.77937pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.77937pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Z

where
{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.77937pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.77937pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
are projections, we obtain

P_{e}(\delta_{Z})=P_{<{\mathrm{pr}_{1}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}>}(\delta_{A})\wedge P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B})=P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B})

P_{e}(\delta_{Z})\leq P_{e}P_{j\times j}P_{[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]\times[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]}(\delta_{A})

Note that
e
can be obtained by the following composition

\textstyle{A\times(A\times A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{<\mathrm{id}_{A},i_{A}>\times(\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{A})}
\textstyle{(A\times(A+B))\times(A\times(A+B))}
\textstyle{(A\times A)\times A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\times A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{e}
\textstyle{\Delta_{A}\times\mathrm{id}_{A}}

Therefore

([{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]\times[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}])(j\times j)e
is equal to

([{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]j<\mathrm{id}_{A},i_{A}>\times[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]j(\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{A}))(\Delta_{A}\times\mathrm{id}_{A})

since
[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]j(\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{A})={\mathrm{pr}_{2}}:A\times A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
one has

([{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]j<\mathrm{id}_{A},i_{A}>\times[{\mathrm{pr}_{2}},{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}]j(\mathrm{id}_{A}\times i_{A}))(\Delta_{A}\times\mathrm{id}_{A})=\mathrm{id}_{A\times A}

whence

P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B})=P_{e}(\delta_{Z})\leq\delta_{A}
.
∎

We now aim at proving that the equality on a coproduct is given by the formula

\delta_{A+B}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{B})

To this purpose we give the following definition.

6.36 Definition
.

Let
P
is a variational f.o.d. on a category
\mathpzc{C}
with
binary distributive coproducts.
Two arrows
h:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
and
k:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
are
jointly
{P}
-surjective

if

\top_{Y}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k}(\top_{X})

6.37 Proposition
.

Canonical injections are jointly
{P}
-surjective
.

Proof.

Suppose
A
and
B
are objects of
\mathpzc{C}
and consider their coproduct with canonical injections
i_{A}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
and
i_{B}:B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
. Abbreviate by
k:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
the weak comprehension

\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}}(\top_{B})}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B

Clearly

\top_{A}\leq P_{i_{A}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}}(\top_{A}))\leq P_{i_{A}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}}(\top_{B}))

and analogously for
i_{B}
. By the universal property of comprehensions there are arrows
t_{A}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
and
t_{B}:B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
with
kt_{A}=i_{A}
and
kt_{B}=i_{B}
. These induce an arrow
[t_{A},t_{B}]:A+B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}X
which is a section of
k
. Thus
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\top_{A+B}}|\kern-2.5pt\}=\mathrm{id}_{A+B}
factors through
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}}(\top_{B})}|\kern-2.5pt\}
. Fullness of comprehensions completes the proof.
∎

6.38 Proposition
.

For every pair of
{P}
-injective
arrows
h:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
and
k:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Y
, for every reflexive relation
\rho
over
A
and every reflexive relation
\theta
over
X
, the following relation over
Y

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho)\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k\times k}(\theta)

is reflexive if and only if
h
and
k
are jointly surjective.

Proof.

Suppose
h
and
k
are jointly surjective and consider the relation
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho)
. By Beck-Chevalley conditions it is

P_{\Delta_{Y}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho))=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}(P_{\Delta_{A}}(\rho))=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}(\top_{A})

Analogously
P_{\Delta_{Y}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k\times k}(\theta))=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k}(\top_{X}))
whence

P_{\Delta_{Y}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho)\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k\times k}(\theta))=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k}(\top_{X})

Therefore, reflexitivity holds,
i.e.

\top_{Y}=P_{\Delta_{Y}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho)\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k\times k}(\theta))

if and only if
h
and
k
are jointly surjective,
i.e.

\top_{Y}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h}(\top_{A})\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{k}(\top_{X})
.
∎

6.39 Proposition
.

For every
A
and
B
in
\mathpzc{C}
it is

\delta_{A+B}=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\delta_{A}\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\delta_{B}

where
i_{A}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
and
i_{B}:B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A+B
are canonical injections.

Proof.

From
\delta_{A}\leq P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B})
and
\delta_{B}\leq P_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})
one obtains a canonical inequality

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\delta_{A}\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\delta_{B}\leq\delta_{A+B}

This is actually an equality, since injections are
{P}
-injective
(
6.35
) and jointly surjective (
6.37
) hence
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\delta_{A}\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\delta_{B}
is reflexive by
6.38
.
∎

We leave to the readers the proof of the following instrumental lemma before the main statement of the section.

6.40 Proposition
.

If
\rho
in
P(A\times A)
is transitive and if
h:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
is
{P}
-injective
, then
\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{h\times h}(\rho)
is transitive.

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

We can finally focus on the elementary quotient completion of a doctrine whose base has distributive binary coproducts.
First recall the following definition.

6.41 Definition
.

Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has finite coproducts. Coproducts are said
distributive
if the canonical arrow

(A\times B)+(A\times C)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.12491pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A\times(B+C)
is an isomorphism.

6.42 Theorem
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.43045pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a variational first-order doctrine.

\mathpzc{C}
has distributive binary coproducts if and only if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has distributive binary coproducts.

Proof.

Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
has binary distributive coproducts. Consider
(A,\rho)
and
(B,\sigma)
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
and the coproduct
A+B
with canonical injections
i_{A}
and
i_{B}
. Define

\rho\boxplus\sigma=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\rho\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\sigma

by lemmas
6.38
and
6.37
the relation
\rho\boxplus\sigma
is reflexive. It is trivially symmetric. Transitivity follows from
6.40
and
6.35
. Thus

\textstyle{(A,\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[i_{A}]}
\textstyle{(A+B,\rho\boxplus\sigma)}
\textstyle{(B,\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[i_{B}]}

is a diagram in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
. We claim that it is a coproduct diagram of
(A,\rho)
and
(B,\sigma)
. It is immediate to see that it is a weak coproduct. We now prove that it is a strong coproduct. Suppose that
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}T
and
g:B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}T
represent two arrows
[f]:(A,\rho)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(T,\theta)
and
[g]:(B,\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(T,\theta)
. If
k
and
l
represents two arrows
[k],[l]:(A+B,\rho\boxplus\sigma)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(T,\theta)
with

\begin{array}[]{c@{\quad\mbox{and}\quad}c}[k][i_{A}]=[f]&[k][i_{B}]=[g]\\[4.30554pt]
[l][i_{A}]=[f]&[l][i_{B}]=[g]\end{array}

i.e.

k
and
l
are such that

\begin{array}[]{c@{\quad\mbox{and}\quad}c}\top_{A}\leq P_{<ki_{A},f>}(\theta)&\top_{B}\leq P_{<ki_{B},g>}(\theta)\\[4.30554pt]
\top_{A}\leq P_{<li_{A},f>}(\theta)&\top_{B}\leq P_{<li_{B},g>}(\theta)\end{array}

then we have also

\rho\leq P_{ki_{A}\times f}(\theta)\wedge P_{li_{A}\times f}(\theta)\leq P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}P_{k\times l}(\theta)
and similarly

\sigma\leq P_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}P_{k\times l}(\theta)
. Therefore

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\rho\leq P_{k\times l}(\theta)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\sigma\leq P_{k\times l}(\theta)

from which
\rho\boxplus\sigma\leq P_{k\times l}(\theta)
,
i.e.

[k]=[l]
.

For the converse, consider
A
and
B
in
\mathpzc{C}
. The coproduct in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
of

(A,\delta_{A})
and
(B,\delta_{B})
is
(A+B,\delta_{A}\boxplus\delta_{B})
. By

6.42
this is
(A+B,\delta_{A+B})
. The claim follows by

6.2
-2.
∎

6.43 Definition
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a variational first-order doctrine whose base has finite coprroducts.
For objects
A
and
B
in the base,
we say that
A+B
is
P
-disjoint if

P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})=\bot_{A\times B}

6.44 Proposition
.

\mathpzc{C}
has
P
-disjoint distributive binary coproducts if and only if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has
\widehat{P}
-disjoint distributive binary coproducts.

Proof.

After
6.42
we only have to prove that coproducts in
\mathpzc{C}
are
P
-disjoint if and only if coproduct in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
are
\widehat{P}
-disjoint. The necessary condition is immediate after
6.39
. Consider the coproduct diagram in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}

\textstyle{(A,\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[i_{A}]}
\textstyle{(A+B,\rho\boxplus\sigma)}
\textstyle{(B,\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[i_{B}]}

it holds

P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\rho\boxplus\sigma)=P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\rho\lor\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\sigma)

but, denoting by
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
the projections from
A\times B\times(A+B)\times(A+B)
,
P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}\rho)
is equal to

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}[P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(P_{i_{A}\times i_{A}}(\delta_{A+B}))\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{4}>}(P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B}))\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{3},\mathrm{pr}_{4}>}(\rho)]

which is equal to
\bot_{A\times B}
under the assumption that
P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})
is so. Analogously
P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{i_{B}\times i_{B}}\sigma)=\bot_{A\times B}
whence the claim.
∎

6.45 Corollary
.

Suppose
\mathpzc{C}
is such that for every
A
the domain of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\bot_{A}}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is an initial object. Then
\mathpzc{C}
has disjoint distributive binary coproducts if and only if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has disjoint distributive binary coproducts.

6.3
Classifiers

In this section we show how the elementary quotient completion of a suitable doctrine
P
inherits a predicate classifier for
the doctrine of strong monomorphisms which coincides with the doctrine
\widehat{P}
.

We first need to establish the following characterisation of epimorphisms as
{P}
-surjective
arrows.

6.46 Lemma
.

Suppose
P
is an existential variational doctrine with a strong predicate classifier as defined in in
2.18
. An arrow
e:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is epic if and only if it is
{P}
-surjective
.

Proof.

We have already observed that
{P}
-surjective
arrows are epimorphism. So let
e:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
be epic. Consider the arrows
\chi_{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.90417pt_{e}(\top_{X})},\chi_{\top_{A}}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
.
Then

P_{e}(P_{\chi_{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.64583pt_{e}(\top_{X})}}(\in))=P_{e}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{e}(\top_{X}))=\top_{X}=P_{e}(\top_{A})=P_{e}(P_{\chi_{\top_{A}}}(\in))

Since the classifier is strong
\chi_{\top_{A}}e=\chi_{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.90417pt_{e}(\top_{X})}e
and then
\chi_{\top_{A}}=\chi_{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.90417pt_{e}(\top_{X})}
as
e
is epic, whence
\top_{A}=P_{\chi_{\top_{A}}}(\epsilon)=P_{\chi_{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-0.64583pt_{e}(\top_{X})}}(\epsilon)=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{e}(\top_{X})
.
∎

Recall that given two factorization systems
(E,M)
and
(E^{\prime},M^{\prime})
on the same category
\mathpzc{C}
, we have that
E=E^{\prime}
if and only if
M=M^{\prime}
. Thus, the following is an immediate corollary of
6.46
and
2.41
.

6.47 Proposition
.

In every m-variational existential doctrine with a strong predicate classifier comprehension arrows are the strong monomorphisms of the base.

Proof.

By
2.41
comprehension arrows are the class of arrows which are right orthogonal to
{P}
-surjective
arrows, but these coincide with epimorphisms by
6.46
, whence the claim.
∎

6.48 Proposition
.

In every m-variational existential doctrine with a strong predicate classifier the domain
T
of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}:T\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
is a terminal object.

Proof.

Let
1
be terminal. The arrow
\chi_{\top_{1}}:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
is such that
P_{\chi_{\top_{1}}}(\in)=\top_{1}
. The universal property of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}
produces an arrow
k:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.77882pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}T
with
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}k=\chi_{\top_{1}}
. The universal property of
1
ensures that
!_{T}k=\mathrm{id}_{1}
. Moreover from

\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}(\in)=\top_{T}=P_{!_{T}}(\top_{1})=P_{!_{T}}P_{\chi_{\top_{!}}}(\in)

we have
\chi_{\top_{1}}!_{T}=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}
, so
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}k!_{T}=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}
, whence
k!_{T}=\mathrm{id}_{T}
as
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is monic.
∎

6.49 Corollary
.

If
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a m-variational existential doctrine, then

P
has a strong predicate classifier if and only if

\mathpzc{C}
has a classifier of strong monomorphism.

Proof.

Suppose
\Omega
is a strong predicate classifier in
P
. After
6.47
it suffices to show that
\Omega
is a classifier for the class of comprehension arrows. Note that every
\alpha
in
P(A)
the following is a pullback

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
\textstyle{!}
\textstyle{T\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\chi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\Omega}

where
!:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}T
is the arrow produced by the universal property of
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}
as
\top_{X}=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\alpha)=P_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}P_{\chi_{\alpha}}(\in)=P_{\chi_{\alpha}\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\in)
.
The object
T
is a terminal (
6.48
). If
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
makes the square
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\in}|\kern-2.5pt\}!=f\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
a pullback, then
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}
is isomorphic to
\{\kern-2.5pt|{P_{f}(\in)}|\kern-2.5pt\}
by lemma
2.32
. By fullness of comprehension
P_{f}(\in)=\alpha=P_{\chi_{\alpha}}(\in)
, hence
f=\chi_{\alpha}
.

Conversely suppose
t:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
is a strong monomorphism classifier. Define

\in=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{t}(\top_{1})
.
For
\alpha
in
P(A)
the arrow
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is a strong monic by
6.47
. Thus there is a unique
\chi_{\alpha}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.91489pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
that makes the following

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{t}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\chi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\Omega}

a pullback. By
2.31
and
2.30
it is
P_{\chi_{\alpha}}(\in)=P_{\chi_{\alpha}}\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{t}(\top_{1})=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}P_{!_{X}}(\top_{1})=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})=\alpha
.
Suppose
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
is such that
P_{f}(\in)=\alpha
.
Since
t:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\Omega
is a strong monomorphism classifier its pullback along
f
classifies a strong monomorphism. By
6.47
this is of the form
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\beta}|\kern-2.5pt\}:X^{\prime}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
. Moreover, by
2.31

\alpha=P_{f}(\in)\ \equiv\,P_{f}(\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{t}(\top_{1}))=\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\beta}|\kern-2.5pt\}}(\top_{X})=\beta
.
So
\{\kern-2.5pt|{\alpha}|\kern-2.5pt\}=\{\kern-2.5pt|{\beta}|\kern-2.5pt\}
and hence
\chi_{\alpha}
and
f
classifies
the same strong monomorphisms showing that
f=\chi_{\alpha}
.
∎

6.50 Proposition
.

Let
P
be a variational first-order doctrine on
\mathpzc{C}
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

P
has a weak predicate classifier;

(ii)

\widehat{P}
has a strong predicate classifier;

(iii)

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has a classifier for strong monomorphisms.

Proof.

1\Leftrightarrow 2
. The necessary condition is immediate, while if
\Omega
is a predicate weak classifier in
\mathpzc{C}
, then
(\Omega,\lambda)
is a predicate classifier in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
where

\lambda=P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\in)\leftrightarrow P_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\in)

2\Leftrightarrow 3
. By
3.13
the doctrine
\widehat{P}
is m-variational. Then apply
6.49
.
∎

7
The quasi-topos construction from a hyper-tripos

In this section we are going to show how the elementary quotient completion
of a suitable tripos gives rise to a quasi-topos completion.

We start by recalling the definition of quasi-topos.

7.1 Definition
.

A
quasi-topos
is a category
\mathpzc{C}
with the following properties:

(i)

it has finite limits;

(ii)

it has finite co-limits;

(iii)

it is locally cartesian closed;

(iv)

there is a classifier for strong monomorphisms.

Then recall from
[
Wyl91
]
that
every quasi-topos has effective quotients of strong equivalence relations,
namely a
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
-equivalence relation represented by a strong
monomorphism. Therefore it makes sense to try to characterise those
quasi-toposes which arise as elementary quotient completions.

In the following when we refer to an equivalence relation in a category

\mathpzc{C}
we mean a
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
-equivalence relation, while for a strong
equivalence relation we mean a
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
-equivalence relation
represented by a strong monomorphism.

If
\mathpzc{C}
is a quasi-topos, then both
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
and
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
are
first-order doctrines. Another first-order doctrine is the doctrine of strong subobjects of

\mathpzc{C}
denoted by
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
.

7.2 Definition
.

A
intensional hyper-tripos
is an elementary existential doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
with a weak predicate classifier and full weak comprehensions such that
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks, is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed and has finite distributive coproducts. A
hyper-tripos
is a intensional hyper-tripos with comprehensive diagonals.

7.3 Proposition
.

A intensional hyper-tripos is a tripos.

Proof.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a intensional hyper-tripos. Then it has full weak comprehensions. By prop.
3.8
there is a topology
j
on
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
such that
P
is
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}j}
. Since
\mathpzc{C}
has weak pullbacks, is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed and has finite distributive coproducts, the doctrine
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
is first-order, so is
P
by prop.
3.6
.
\mathpzc{C}
is weakly cartesian closed and
P
has a weak classifier, so
P
has weak power objects by prop.
2.19
.
∎

7.4 Proposition
.

A hyper-tripos has
P
-disjoint coproducts.

Proof.

Take two objects
A,B

and consider
\chi_{\top_{A}}:A\rightarrow\Omega
and
\chi_{\perp_{B}}:B\rightarrow\Omega
.
Abbreviate with
d:A+B\rightarrow\Omega
the arrow
[\chi_{\top_{A}},\chi_{\perp_{B}}]
.
From
\delta_{A+B}\leq P_{d\times d}(\delta_{\Omega})

we get

P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})\leq P_{di_{A}\times di_{B}}(\delta_{\Omega})=\chi_{\top_{A}}\times\chi_{\perp_{B}}(\delta_{\Omega})
.
Finally observe that
\delta_{\Omega}\leq P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\in)\leftrightarrow P_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\in)
from which
we deduce

\chi_{\top_{A}}\times\chi_{\perp_{B}}(\delta_{\Omega})\ \leq P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(P_{\chi_{\top_{A}}}(\in))\ \leftrightarrow P_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(P_{\chi_{\top_{A}}}(\in))=P_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\top_{A})\leftrightarrow P_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(\perp_{B})=\top_{A\times B}\leftrightarrow\perp_{A\times B}=\perp_{A\times B}
. One concludes that

P_{i_{A}\times i_{B}}(\delta_{A+B})\leq\perp_{A\times B}
.
∎

7.5 Proposition
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be a tripos. For every
A
and every
\rho
in
P(A\times A)
there is a
P
-equivalence relation
\overline{\rho}
over
A
such that
\rho\leq\overline{\rho}
and for every
P
-equivalence relation
\mu
over
A
, if
\rho\leq\sigma
then
\mu\leq\overline{\rho}
.

Proof.

We shall employ the language introduced in
2.24
. Take
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and define the formulas in
U:\mathbb{P}{A\times A}

\textsf{r}(U):=\forall_{a:A}(a,a)\in_{A}U

\textsf{s}(U):=\forall_{a:A}\forall_{a^{\prime}:A}[(a,a^{\prime})\in_{A}U\Rightarrow(a^{\prime},a)\in_{A}U]

\textsf{t}(U):=\forall_{a:A}\forall_{a^{\prime}:A}\forall_{a^{\prime\prime}:A}[((a,a^{\prime})\in_{A}U\wedge(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\in_{A}U)\Rightarrow(a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime})\in_{A}U]

\textsf{eq}(U):=\textsf{r}(U)\wedge\textsf{s}(U)\wedge\textsf{t}(U)

For every formula
\rho
over
A\times A
define
\overline{\rho}
to be the following

a:A,a^{\prime}:A\mid\forall_{U:\mathbb{P}{(A\times A)}}[\textsf{eq}(U)\wedge\forall_{x:A}\forall_{x^{\prime}:A}(\rho(x,x^{\prime})\Rightarrow(x,x^{\prime})\in_{A}U)\Rightarrow(a,a^{\prime})\in_{A}U]

It is an easy exercise in first-order logic to check that
\overline{\rho}
is a
P
-equivalence relation over
A\times A
. Then, take any
\mu
in
P(A\times A)
and consider
\chi_{\mu}:1\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathbb{P}{(A\times A)}
. Recall that
\chi_{\mu}
has the property that
(a,a^{\prime})\in_{A}\chi_{\mu}\dashv\vdash\mu(a,a^{\prime})
, so in
a:A,a^{\prime}:A
it holds

\overline{\rho}(a,a^{\prime})\vdash\textsf{eq}(\chi_{\mu})\wedge\forall_{x:A}\forall_{x^{\prime}:A}(\rho(x,x^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mu(x,x^{\prime}))\Rightarrow\mu(a,a^{\prime})

If
\mu
is a
P
-equivalence relation over
A
then
\textsf{eq}(\chi_{\mu})
is a true sentence. If moreover
\rho\leq\mu
also
\forall_{x:A}\forall_{x^{\prime}:A}(\rho(x,x^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mu(x,x^{\prime}))
is a true sentence. Whence the sequent above reduces to
\overline{\rho}(a,a^{\prime})\vdash\mu(a,a^{\prime})
, which proves the claim.
∎

7.6 Lemma
.

The base of a tripos with effective quotients and comprehensive diagonals has coequalizers.

Proof.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be a tripos with quotients. We shall employ the language introduced in
2.24
. Take two arrows
f,g:Y\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and define
\rho
in
P(A\times A)
to be

a:A,a^{\prime}:A\mid\exists_{y:Y}(f(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a\wedge g(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a^{\prime})

By
7.5
there is the smallest
P
-equivalence relation
\overline{\rho}
over
A
that contains
\rho
. Consider the quotient
q:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A/\overline{\rho}
. It is clear that

y^{\prime}:Y\mid\exists_{y:Y}f(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}f(y^{\prime})\wedge g(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}g(y^{\prime})

is a true formula, that is to say
\top_{Y}\leq P_{\langle f,g\rangle}(\rho)
. So also
\top_{Y}\leq P_{\langle f,g\rangle}(\overline{\rho})
and by effectiveness of quotient
\top_{Y}\leq P_{\langle qf,qg\rangle}(\delta_{A/\overline{\rho}})
. So
qf=qg
as
P
has comprehensive diagonals.

Suppose now
k:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Z
is such that
kf=kg
, then in
y:Y,a:A,a^{\prime}:A
it holds

f(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a\wedge g(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a^{\prime}\vdash kf(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}k(a)\wedge kg(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}k(a^{\prime})\vdash k(a)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}k(a^{\prime})

Therefore
\exists_{y:Y}(f(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a\wedge g(y)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}a^{\prime}\vdash k(a)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{A}}k(a^{\prime})
. That is to say that
\rho\leq P_{k\times k}(\delta_{A})
. By
7.5
also
\overline{\rho}\leq P_{k\times k}(\delta_{A})
. By the universal property of quotients there is
h:A/\overline{\rho}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}Z
with
hq=k
.
∎

7.7 Definition
.

Let
\mathpzc{C}
be a quasi-topos. We denote with
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
the doctrine of strong subobjects of
\mathpzc{C}
, namely equivalence classes up to isomorphisms of those monic orthogonal to epimorphisms in
\mathpzc{C}
.

From
[
Wyl91
]
we can easily deduce that:

7.8 Lemma
.

The doctrine of strong subobjects
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
of a quasi-topos
\mathpzc{C}
is a tripos.

7.9 Proposition
.

Let be
\mathpzc{C}
a quasi-topos. The following are
equivalent:

(i)

the quasi-topos
\mathpzc{C}
is a topos;

(ii)

the doctrine of strong subobjects
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}
satisfies
(RUC)
;

(iii)

the doctrine strong subobjects
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}
is the subobject
doctrine
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}
.

Proof.

A quasi-topos
\mathpzc{C}
is a topos if and only if it is balanced. By prop.
2.33
a monic arrow in
\mathpzc{C}
is
{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}
-injective
and by
6.46
epimorphisms are
{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}
-surjective
. Then, the equivalences follow by prop.
2.36
. ∎

7.10 Theorem
.

A doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a hyper-tripos if and only if

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a quasi-topos and
\widehat{P}
is the doctrine of strong subobjects.

Proof.

In the def.
7.1
of quasi-topos, point (i) comes from prop.
3.14
, point (ii) from prop.
6.32
, while point (iv) comes from prop.
6.50
. Finite coproducts come directly from prop.
6.44
and the existence of co-equalizers
from lemma
7.6
. In particular the initial object in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is the initial object of
\mathpzc{C}
with the total relation. By prop.
3.13
the doctrine
\widehat{P}:\mathpzc{Q}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is m-variational. Hence
\widehat{P}
is the doctrine of strong monomorphisms of
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
by prop.
6.47
.

For the converse, if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a quasi-topos, by lemma
7.8

Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}
is a tripos and it coincides with
\widehat{P}
by prop.
6.47
. So by prop
6.1
and theorem
6.32

P
is first-order on a slice-wise weakly cartesian closed category with a weak predicated classifier by prop.
6.50
. Whence
P
is a tripos. Coproducts follows from prop.
6.44
.
∎

7.11 Corollary
.

If
P
is a intensional hyper-tripos then
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a quasi-topos and also
{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
is a hyper-tripos.

Proof.

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a quasi-topos with the same proof in
7.10
as comprehensive diagonals in
P
play no role in the proof. Moreover,
from theorem
2.61
we know that
\widehat{P}
is equivalent to
\widehat{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
,
and hence we conclude that
{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
is hyper-tripos and hence from theorem
7.10
.
∎

As a corollary we also get Menni’s characterization of toposes as exact
completions in
[
Men03
]
as follows.

7.12 Corollary
.

For a finite product category
\mathpzc{C}
with weak pullbacks,

\mathpzc{C}
is slice-wise cartesian closed and has a weak proof classifier if and only if
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is a topos.

Proof.

It follows by theorem
7.10
and prop
7.9
when
P=\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
after recalling that
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
is a hyper-tripos precisely when
\mathpzc{C}
is slice-wise cartesian closed and has a weak proof classifier as remarked in ex.
(d)
of
2.22
and that
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is equivalent to
\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}
as remarked in ex.
(d)
of
2.54
.

∎

Denote by
\mathcal{T}_{P}
the topos that comes from the tripos
P
under the tripos to topos construction. If
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a hyper-tripos (intensional hyper-tripos), then
\mathcal{T}_{P}\equiv\mathpzc{EF}_{\widehat{P}}
(this is theorem 3.5 in
[
MPR17
]
). Thus for every hyper-tripos
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.21523pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
the topos
\mathcal{T}_{P}
is the topos of coarse objects of
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
.

Note that from the proof of theorem
7.10
that comprehensive diagonals are not necessary to get a quasi-topos out of the elementary quotient completion.

Theorem
7.10
can be extended to produce arithmetic quasi-toposes.

7.13 Definition
.

A quasi-topos is
arithmetic
if it has a natural number object, and hence a parameterized natural numbers object.

7.14 Proposition
.

P
is a intensional hyper-tripos with a natural numbers object) if and only if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a arithmetic quasi-topos.

Proof.

From theorem
7.10
and lemma 3.5. of
[
MPR19
]
stating we know that if
P
has a parameterized natural number objects if and only if
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
has a parameterized natural number object.
∎

Recall from
[
Joh02
]
that

7.15 Definition
.

An object
A
in a category
\mathpzc{C}
is
coarse
if for every morphism
f:C\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
which is both monic and epic and
every
g:C\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
there is a unique
t:B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.90968pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
such that
g=tf
.

7.16 Proposition
.

Every quasi-topos
\mathpzc{C}
contains a full reflective subcategory
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
which is a topos.

Proof.

The topos
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
is reflective and the reflector is build as follows. For any object
A
note that the diagonal
\delta_{A}
in
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}(A\times A)
is represented by the diagonal (being the diagonal a strong monomorphism).
Its classifying arrow
\chi_{\delta_{A}}
factors as a strong monic followed by an epimorphism as

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\chi_{\delta_{A}}}
\textstyle{\eta_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}{A}}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{S}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\kern-2.5pt|{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\chi_{\delta_{A}}}\top_{A}}|\kern-2.5pt\}}

The object
\mathpzc{S}_{A}
will be called
object of
A
-singletons
and we call
