\eta_{A}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{S}_{A}
the
singleton arrow
of
A
. Note that
\chi_{\delta_{A}}
is monic, whence the singleton arrow of
A
is both epic and monic. Strong monic are strong comprehension arrows in
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}
so it is easy to see that every arrow
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
determines a unique arrow
\mathpzc{S}_{f}:\mathpzc{S}_{A}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{S}_{B}
with
\mathpzc{S}_{f}i_{A}=i_{B}f
. This determines a functor
\mathpzc{S}:\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{C}
where
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
is the full subcategory of
\mathpzc{C}
on coarse objects.

As shown in
[
Joh02
, prop 2.6.12]
the functor
\mathpzc{S}
is left adjoint to the inclusion of
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
into
\mathpzc{C}
with singleton arrows as unite. So in particular an object
A
is coarse if and only if it is isomorphic to its own singletons,
i.e.
if
A\simeq\mathpzc{S}_{A}
.
∎

Recall from remark
2.60
that the construction that maps an
existential elementary doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
to the existential
elementary doctrine
P_{F}:\mathpzc{EF}_{P}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
satisfying (RUC).

7.17 Proposition
.

Let
\mathpzc{C}
be a quasi-topos.
The doctrine of strong subobjects
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
along the
inclusion of the topos of coarse objects
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
is equivalent to

\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}}:\mathpzc{EF}_{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
.

Proof.

Any arrow
m
in
\mathpzc{C}
can be written as the composite
m=se
where
s
is
strong monic and
e
is epic. If
m
is monic then
e
is monic too. So if

m
is monic in
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
then
e
is an isomorphism. So every monic in

\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
so the change of base of
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}
along the inclusion of

\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
into
\mathpzc{C}
is

\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}}:\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.79858pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
. So it suffices to show that

\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}
is equivalent to
\mathpzc{EF}_{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}
.

We want to find a functor
S^{\prime}:\mathpzc{EF}_{Stg_{\mathpzc{Q}}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.49304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{Q}}

naturally inverse to the composition

\textstyle{\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{C}}\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{EF}_{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}}

where
\Gamma_{Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}}
acts as the identity on objects and maps

f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
to the formula
a:A,b:B\mid f(a)\mathrel{\mathord{=}_{B}}b
in
Stg_{\mathpzc{C}}(A\times B)
. On the object we define
S^{\prime}
as the action of the reflector

S:\mathpzc{Q}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{Q}}
in lemma
7.17
i.e for any object
A

we put
S^{\prime}A=SA
. To define the inverse on morphisms take a total and
single-valued relation
F\in Stg_{\mathpzc{Q}}(A\times B)
, this is a strong monic

F=<F_{1},F_{2}>:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A\times B
in
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
where
F_{1}:X\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is monic and
epic. Thus
SF_{1}:SX\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}SA
is an isomorphism. Finally define
S^{\prime}F=SF_{2}(SF_{1})^{-1}
.
∎

We can characterize when an hyper-tripos leads to an elementary quotient
completion which is a topos.

7.18 Theorem
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be a hyper-tripos. Then the following are equivalent.

(i)

P
satisfies
(RC)
.

(ii)

\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a topos and coincides with the exact completion of the
base of
P
as a finite product category.

Proof.

By prop.
2.37

P
is
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
if and only if
(1)
holds. Hence the equivalence follows by
7.12
.
∎

Therefore, examples of toposes arising in this way are exactly those obtained
as exact completions of a category
\mathpzc{C}
as in
[
Men03
]
by
taking
\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
for
P
.

Furthermore, we characterize those elementary quotient completions which
arise as tripos-to-topos constructions originally introduced in

[
HJP80
]
.

To this purpose, recall from
[
MPR17
]
the following definition.

7.19 Definition
.

We say that an elementary existential doctrine
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

is
equipped with
\epsilon
-operators
if
for any object
A
in
\mathpzc{C}
and any

\alpha
in
P(A\times B)
there exists an arrow

\epsilon_{\alpha}:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
such that

\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}}(\alpha)=P_{<\mathrm{id}_{A},\epsilon_{\alpha}>}(\alpha)

holds in
P(A)
, where
{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}:A\times B\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}A
is the first projection.

7.20 Definition
.

Given a tripos
P
,
let us denote with
\tau_{P}
the tripos-to-topos construction
the category
\mathpzc{T}_{P}
consists of

objects:

pairs
\langle A,\rho\rangle
such that
\rho
is in

P(A\times A)
and satisfies symmetry and transitivity as in
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
2.42
.

arrows:

an arrow
\phi:\langle A,\rho\rangle\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.13191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\langle B,\sigma\rangle
is an
object
\phi
in
P(A\times B)
such that

(i)

\phi\leq P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{1}>}(\rho)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\sigma)
;

(ii)

P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\rho)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\phi)\leq P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\phi)
in
P(A\times A\times B)

where the
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
’s are the projections from
A\times A\times B
;

(iii)

P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\phi)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\sigma)\leq P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\phi)
in
P(A\times B\times B)

where the
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
’s are the projections from
A\times B\times B
;

(iv)

P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{2}>}(\phi)\wedge P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{1},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\phi)\leq P_{<\mathrm{pr}_{2},\mathrm{pr}_{3}>}(\sigma)
in
P(A\times B\times B)

where the
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
’s are as in (iii);

(v)

P_{<\mathrm{id}_{A},\mathrm{id}_{A}>}(\rho)\leq\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}\kern-1.29167pt_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}(\phi)

in
P(A)

where the
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
’s are the projections from
A\times B
.

7.21 Theorem
.

Let
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
be a tripos. Then the following are equivalent.

(i)

P
is equipped with
\epsilon
-operators.

(ii)

{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}
satisfies
(RC)
.

(iii)

\mathpzc{Q}_{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}}
is a topos and coincides with the tripos-to-topos
construction
\tau_{P}
of the tripos
P
.

Proof.

(i) and (ii) are equivalent by theorem 5.15 in
[
MPR17
]
where the category of
predicates
{{\mathpzc{Prd}\kern-1.72218pt_{{P}}}}
denotes
\mathpzc{X}_{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}}
.
To show that (ii) implies (iii) observe that from theorem 5.5 in
[
MPR17
]

we know that
{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}
satisfies the rule of choice iff
{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
satisfies
the rule of choice.
By theorem
7.18
,
we also know that
{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
satisfies the rule of choice iff

\mathpzc{Q}_{{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
is a topos and coincides with the exact completion of
the base of
{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}
. Moreover
\mathpzc{Q}_{{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
is the exact completion of
the base of
{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}

iff
\mathpzc{Q}_{{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
is equivalent to
\tau_{P}
by corollary 6.3 in

[
MPR17
]
.
From
[
MR13a
]
we know that
\mathpzc{Q}_{{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize x}}}}}
is equivalent
to
\mathpzc{Q}_{{P_{{}_{\textrm{\scriptsize c}}}}}
and this concludes the proof.
∎

7.22 Theorem
.

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.43745pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a intensional hyper-tripos.
Its tripos-to-topos construction
\mathpzc{T}_{P}
is a reflective subcategory of the quasi-topos
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
and coincides with
the category of coarse objects of
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}

\textstyle{\mathpzc{Q}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{T}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(10)

Proof.

It follows by theorem
7.17
, theorem
7.10
and theorem 4.7
of
[
MPR17
]
.
∎

8
Applications

Suppose
P:\mathpzc{C}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.8958pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.8958pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a intensional hyper-tripos. The category
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is a quasi-topos by
7.11
. Its reflective subcategory on coarse objects
\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{Q}_{P}}
is the topos
\mathpzc{T}_{P}
obtained from
P
via the tripos-to-topos construction by theorem
7.22
.
Moreover, by prop.
3.5

and prop.
3.4
, we know that the category
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is also a full reflective
subcategory of
\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}
and that
\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}
is equivalent to
\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}

as summarized in this picture

\textstyle{\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{Q}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{T}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(11)

By prop.
3.5
the intensional hyper-tripos
P
generates a topology
j_{P}
on

\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}
whose extension
\widehat{j_{P}}
is a topology on
\mathop{{}\mathrm{Sub}}_{\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}}
.
Moreover,
\mathpzc{Q}_{P}
is equivalent to
{\textsf{Sep}}(\widehat{j_{P}})
by
prop.
3.16
. So picture
11
can be equivalently
described as follows:

\textstyle{\mathpzc{Q}_{\Psi_{\mathpzc{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{Q}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{T}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{C}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{{\textsf{Sep}}(\widehat{j_{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\textsf{Crs}_{\mathpzc{Q}_{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

We now instantiate this picture on differente choices of
P
.

Recall triposes of the form
\mathcal{P}{H}
as
2.22
-(b). As observed in
prop.
3.5

these triposes have comprehensions but they are not full. We can then consider the completion
\mathcal{P}_{\mathpzc{H}c}:\mathpzc{Set}_{c}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.81252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.81252pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
described in
3.5
.
Note that
\mathpzc{Set}_{c}
is Goguen’s category
{\textsf{Fuz}}(\mathpzc{H})
of
\mathpzc{H}
-valued fuzzy sets
[
Gog74
]
for a local
\mathpzc{H}
. Whence
\mathpzc{Set}_{c}
is a quasitopos and therefore it is slice-wise cartesian closed with finite coproducts. It can be equivalently described as the category
\mathpzc{H}_{+}
obtained by freely adding coproducts to
\mathpzc{H}

[
Men03
]
. The ex/lex completion of
\mathpzc{H}_{+}
is the topos
{\textsf{PreShv}}(\mathpzc{H})
of presheaves of
\mathpzc{H}
, while the tripos-to-topos construction applied to
P_{c}
gives the topos
{\textsf{Shv}}(\mathpzc{H})
of sheaves over
\mathpzc{H}
. Thus, when
P
is
\mathcal{P}{H}
,
picture
10
becomes

\textstyle{{\textsf{PreShv}}(\mathpzc{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{{\textsf{Sep}}(\widehat{j_{\mathcal{P}_{\mathpzc{H}c}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{{\textsf{Shv}}(\mathpzc{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The change of base of triposes of the form
\mathcal{P}{H}
along the forgetful
functor
\textbf{Top}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.6945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.6945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Set}
is again a tripos as it suffices to endow
\mathpzc{H}^{A}
with
the indiscrete topology to have the power objects (see

[
Pas18
]
). In the special case of
\mathpzc{H}=\{0,1\}
, the tripos

\mathcal{P}{H}
reduces to the contravariant powerset functor
\mathcal{P}:\mathpzc{Set}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}

and its change of base along
\textbf{Top}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.6945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.6945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Set}
is a tripos that we call

\mathpzc{T}
. The tripos
\mathpzc{T}
has full strong comprehensions given by
subspace topologies. Top has coproducts and is slice-wise weakly cartesian closed
[
CR00
]
. So
\mathpzc{T}:\textbf{Top}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
is a hyper-tripos. The
category of generalised equilogical spaces
Gequ
is equivalent to
the base
\mathpzc{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}
of the elementary quotient completion
of
\mathcal{P}
. Since
\mathpzc{T}
is boolean the topology
j{T}
is the double negation topology of example
3.7
, whence also
\widehat{j{T}}
. So picture
10
becomes.

\textstyle{\textbf{Top}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
Gequ
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{Set}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

As a byproduct we have that
Gequ
is the category of
\neg\neg
-separated objects of
\textbf{Top}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
as shown in
[
Ros00
]
.

The category
Asm
of assemblies has as objects are pair
(A,\alpha)
where
A
is a set
\alpha:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathbb{P}{\mathsf{N}}
is a function from a to non-empty subsets of natural numbers. An arrow
f:(A,\alpha)\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(B,\beta)
is a function
f:A\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}B
such that there is
n\in\mathsf{N}
such that for all
a
in
A
and all
p
in
\alpha(a)
the application
n\textbf{.}p
is defined and it belongs to
\beta(f(a))
.The category
Pasm
of partitioned assemblies is the full subcategory of
Asm
on those
(A,\alpha)
such that each
\alpha(a)
is a singleton,
i.e.

\alpha
can be seen as a function from
A
to
\mathsf{N}
. The change of base of
\mathcal{P}
along the forgetful functor
\textsf{Pasm}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\mathpzc{Set}
is a tripos as it suffice to chose has weak power object of
(A,\alpha)
the partitioned assembly
(\mathcal{P}A,k_{0})
where
k_{0}
is the constant map to
0
. We call such a tripos
\mathpzc{R}:\textsf{Pasm}^{\textrm{\scriptsize op}}\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.79163pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.79163pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}{\textsf{POS}}
. It is easy to see that
\mathpzc{R}
has full strong comprehensions where for a partitioned assembly
(A,\alpha)
and a subset
X\subseteq A
the inclusion of of
X
into
A
determines a morphism of partitioned assembly
\{\kern-2.5pt|{X}|\kern-2.5pt\}:(X,\alpha_{|X})\mathrel{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.48608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}(A,\alpha)
which is the desired comprehension arrow. Since
Pasm
is slice-wise cartesian closed, the tripos
\mathpzc{R}
is an hyper-tripos. Whence
\mathpzc{Q}{R}
is a quasi-topos by
7.10
. Recall that
\textsf{Pasm}_{\textrm{\scriptsize ex/lex}}
is
\mathpzc{Eff}
. And the quasitopos of
\neg\neg
-separated objects of
\mathpzc{Eff}
is
Asm
. So picture
10
becomes

\textstyle{\mathpzc{Eff}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bot}
Asm
\textstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathpzc{Set}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

As byproduct we have that
\mathpzc{Q}{R}
is equivalent to
Asm
. A different proof of this is given in
[
MPR19
]
.

Another remarkable example is in type theory with the construction of the so called setoid models over Coquand-Huet’s Calculus of Inductive Constructions
\mathrm{CoC}

[
Coq90
]
.
The setoid model of functional relations over
\mathrm{CoC}
is the tripos-to-topos construction
\mathcal{T}_{F^{\mathrm{CoC}}}
with
F^{\mathrm{CoC}}
the doctrine of propositions over
\mathrm{CoC}
mentioned in
[
MR13b
]
.
The topos
\mathcal{T}_{F^{\mathrm{CoC}}}
coincides with the topos of coarse objects within the quasi-topos
\mathpzc{Q}_{F^{\mathrm{CoC}}}
whic was one of the inspiring examples to the introduction of the elementary quotient completion
in
[
MR13b
]
.

9
Conclusions

We have introduced the notion of
quasi-topos construction
of an
hypertripos
by employing the machinery of the elementary quotient completion
introduced in
[
MR13b
]
,
[
MR13a
]
.

In doing so we have generalized three theorems regarding exact completions by adopting the approach of elementary quotient completions: Carboni-Vitali’s characterization of exact completions in terms of projectives in
[
CV98
]
, Carboni-Rosolini’s characterization
of locally cartesian closed exact completions of a category with finite products and weak
pullbacks in
[
CR00
]
, and Menni’s characterization of topoi as exact completions in
[
Men03
]
.
These relevant examples of elementary quotient completions which are not exact completions like the category of assemblies of realizability topos.

In the future we intend to generalize the quasitopos construction
to include examples like the syntactic models obtained from predicative theories such as the extensional level of the Minimalist Foundation in
[
Mai09
]
.
